## Where things live (2026-10-19 cleanup)
- **Plan of record:** `docs/planning/WEEK_PLAN_2026-10-17.md`.
- **Game rules:** Tyler's rulings of record (memory `rulings-of-record.md`, newest wins). If a rule is not there, it is not ruled.
- **The pre-rebuild board** (b1xx to b585, the scrapped old client and its systems) is archived whole at `docs/archive/planning/PRIORITY_BOARD_pre-rebuild.md`. None of its rows apply to the rebuilt game unless re-added here.

## Decisions
_Tyler's rulings live in rulings-of-record; designer rulings inside them are recorded here._

### 2026-11-04 Art batch 0 and the sample of five (art-director, lane/a-art-batch-0): BUILT by pixel script, 0 generations, 0 spend, read at 1x in the real game. **Tyler: the creature sample below needs your word per spend**
Batches 0 and 1 of the audit's plan below. Shots: `art/review/art-batch-0-2026-11-04/` (`before-after-icons.png`, `tiles-in-family.png`, `family-on-palette-rejected.png`, `bag-grid-x3.png`, and the real bag, bar, Configure action, Shop and Hunts at `desktop/` 1440x900 and `phone/` 844x390).
| Piece | What it is now | Read at the player's size |
|---|---|---|
| Mend, Rend, Protective Ward | framed tiles in the family's own colours (`tools/world-art/ability-tiles.mjs`): a sewn leaf, three gashes, a gold-rimmed shield in a bubble | all 16 abilities are a tile in Configure action (32 px) and on the bar (64 px); the grey glyph is gone. A first Rend, three fat lenses, read as chillies at x2, so it was redrawn thin |
| Bronze-Rimmed Shield | dark planks, a bright dented bronze rim and boss (`tools/world-art/twin-icons.mjs`) | beats the old twin: told from the Practice Shield at once at 1x, by rim color |
| Kingsgrate | a barred iron buckler with three gold-tipped crown teeth, a bronze boss, rust and slime | beats it, and is the strongest of the five: the only shield in the bag that isn't a round. A rare finally looks like the Rat King's |
| Iron Hatchets | bearded iron axe heads on wrapped hafts | beats it: real axes against the practice pair's pale fans |
| Oak Crossbow | a thick iron prod, a loaded bolt, a stout oak stock | beats it: heavier at a glance. Its first bolt, two pixels wide, read as a candle and was cut to one |
| Crown Shard | a snapped piece of the crown's ring: three gilt points, the dark lining and beads, raw iron at the break | beats it (it wore the whole crown): beside the crown it reads as a piece of it. Two earlier drafts were rejected: a grey spearhead, and a bright spike on a band that read as a fez |

**The family call:** the 13 tiles stay off the palette and the 3 new ones match them. On the palette was tried two ways (`family-on-palette-rejected.png`): a straight snap makes the frame patchy red, and it isn't close; a frame re-graded by hand to torch, gold and bronze is calmer and nearer Charcoal & Gold, but `hr-world-56-v2` has no white, so every emblem loses its top step (Power Strike's core, the Barrier's glow), and that isn't clearly better. It's also a pass over shipped art, which Tyler rejected on 10-22. Written into `docs/ART_DIRECTION.md` section 7. If Tyler wants the calmer gold frame, it's a script away and costs nothing: all 16 at once, never half a bar.

**Guard:** `tests/pixel-icons.mjs` now fails on any ability with no tile, and on a drawn icon that differs from its script (`drawn`, 12 planted defects). `KNOWN_TWINS` lost the pairs these five cleared; it's 44 items on 21 pictures now (49 before), 19 drawings left.

**What the sample says about the rest:** hard-edged metal and wood draw well by script. The 19 left split: 7 hard pieces (3 shields, Steel Hatchets, Willow Crossbow, Dented Copper Pot, Coppercap's Cauldron) are the same job and I'd do them next at 0 credits. The 12 soft ones (whisker, sting, stinger, bristle, tusk, tooth, hide, ruff, web, tallow, lash, fleck) are untested by script: fur and sinew are where a rule-drawn icon can go stiff, so 3 of them come first as their own sample, and any that don't beat the near-item picture they wear stay on it or go to PixelLab (`create_image_pro_flash`, docs say 10-25 gens a call, per-icon cost not recorded). **Creatures need PixelLab, Tyler's call:** the sample of 2 (Drain Rat, Granary Rat) is assumed 20-60 gens, all 6 are 60-180 (the audit's assumption, not measured); `get_balance` first. I'd run the 2-rat sample: Cellar to Sewer to Wickstead is the same rat three times, and no icon fixes that.

**Seen, not touched (this brief: no UI changes):** on the phone every Configure action icon draws at 28 px, 0.875x, against the whole-number rule (`src/styles/hunt.css` `.ca-ico`, measured on all 16 tiles); it needs 32 px and a fit check on the phone suite. The Hunts lineup is still cut at the bottom of its pane at 1440x900, so the Kingsgrate's row sits below the fold there (the audit's note). In Configure action, picking a spell grows the sentence above the list, so the list moves under the pointer and Details follows the hover to another row: pick Mend and it describes Power Strike (`desktop/05-configure-mend-picked.png`).

**Done:** 3 tiles, 5 icons, the guard, the spec line. **Verified:** on the local world at 1440x900 and 844x390: every new image loaded, at 32 or 64 px except that phone list, 0 console errors. **Assumed:** that Tyler reads these as I do; nobody but me has seen them. **Could still be wrong:** the sample proves hard objects only, and the five were stocked into the bag by SQL on the local world's in-memory database to read them beside their twins, not found by playing (the Shop's Bronze-Rimmed Shield and the Sewer's rows are the real lists).

### 2026-11-04 Art audit: what a player actually sees (asset-director, lane/a-art-audit): MEASURED on the local world, nothing generated, no spend. **Tyler: three costed batches below, none run**
**The smoke's numbers describe a retired layer.** "161 items still on old art/emoji", "19 withheld", "15 unresolved", "36 awaiting the batch" count the painted Hearthfire/portrait manifests (`item-art.js` SHIPPED, `monster-art.js`), which the rebuilt client never draws (`ITEM_ART_SET = 'pixel'`; creatures come from the style-1 atlases). What a player meets: 573 of 573 items on a pixel icon, 25 of 25 shop supplies, 30 of 30 creatures on the six open grounds on live sprites with all five clips, 0 emoji and 0 glyph standing in for an item. Walked as a fresh hero at 1440x900 (42 states: town, 17 windows, all six Hunts pages, five hunts' views, the Cellar with loot, the bag, Configure action, Shop, a bench, Outfit): 0 emoji lines, 0 broken images, 0 asset 404s, clean console (one 409 from the engine call in the first walk, which skipped time; not an asset). Not walked: phone, the benches beyond Smithing, the Glade's hunt view, 1x zoom. The 29 painted filenames behind the withheld/unresolved counts need no wiring (15 name no live item; 14 are live items with a pixel icon already; painted art may not sit beside style-1). Smoke lines reworded to say so (`tests/run-smoke.mjs`, wording only).

**Reachable today:** 326 of 573 items (`reachableSources()`, the six open grounds, the listed Shop, the start kit, benches from reachable inputs); 292 without the Warrens (Level 32+, past the first 20 hours). The other 247 are later tiers, premium or cut: not a problem.

**What is really visible and wrong, ranked** (shots in `art/review/art-audit-2026-11-04/`):
| # | Gap | Measured | Where a player meets it | To fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | One sprite under several names | 30 creatures on 24 pictures; byte-identical idle frames: Giant Rat = Drain Rat = Granary Rat, Slime = Sump Slime, Hive Wasp = Barn Wasp, Cistern Spider = Barn Spider, Glade Poacher = Turncoat (`same-sprite.png`) | Cellar to Sewer to Wickstead: the same rat three times; 4 of Wickstead's 5 creatures are another ground's | 6 new creatures (2 rats, slime, wasp, spider or its twin, Turncoat) |
| 2 | Items wearing another item's picture | 21 groups, 49 items on 21 pictures (`KNOWN_TWINS`); 45 are reachable; 24 drawings clear every reachable twin, 17 of them outside the Warrens (`twins-*.png`) | Six shields on one picture: the start kit's Practice Shield, the Shop's Bronze-Rimmed Shield, the Rat King's rare Kingsgrate; 3 hatchets, 3 crossbows; Rat King's Crown = Crown Shard; Coppercap's Cauldron drawn as a crown; a Hive Wasp Stinger drawn as a sac | 24 icons (list below) |
| 3 | Glyph beside framed tiles | Mend, Rend, Protective Ward have no tile (13 of 16 do) | Configure action, Spells and Runes tabs; Mend is every hero's heal (`configure-spells-glyphs.png`) | 3 tiles |
| 4 | Ability tiles off the world palette | 13 of 13: 1024 of 1024 pixels opaque and off-palette, 29-60 colours (items: 420 opaque, 13 colours, 0 off) | The bar's first slots from minute one (`bar-slots-x3.png`) | Art Director's call: a coherent family, so I'd keep it and draw the 3 missing in its frame |
| 5 | Idle is one frame | `idle 1f` on all 30 creatures; the bible (section 6) wants 4-6 | Every hunt | later, sample first |
| 6 | Purpose-built screens (bible section 12) | Farm shows two empty dark squares, House is text only | Farm, House | designer and Art Director, not asset work |

Also seen, not art: the Hunts page's lineup is cut at the bottom of its pane at 1440x900 (every creature's level line, and the Sewer's first row shows no names at all; `screens/21-hunt-millrace_sewer.png`). The dead weight: 148 + 488 painted files (about 28 MB) and `assets/items`, `assets/avatars`, `assets/art-pilot` (126 files, about 107 MB) are tracked and never fetched; archiving them also retires the two painted preflights, so it waits on Tyler's word.

**The 24 icons, by exposure** (new picture needed; the first named keeps the old): Shields: Bronze-Rimmed (Shop), Kingsgrate (Rat King), Ironbound and Steelgrate and Coppercap (Smithing), keeping the Practice Shield's. Hatchets: Iron (Sewer drop), Steel (Warrens). Crossbows: Oak (Sewer), Willow (Warrens). Crown Shard. Coppercap's Cauldron (Warrens). Granary Rat Whisker, Barn Wasp Sting, Hive Wasp Stinger, Boar Bristle, Orchard Boar Tusk, Brook Bear Tooth, Giant Rat Hide, Grayjaw's Ruff, Loft Web. Warrens: Coppercap's Tallow, Dented Copper Pot, Glowing Copper Fleck, Foreman's Lash.

**Generation plan, costed, NOT RUN** (section 10: a sample of about 5, played and read, then batches of 20 or fewer):
| Batch | What | Tool | Credits | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0, free | 3 ability tiles (Mend, Rend, Protective Ward) | the pixel scripts, as Cleave was (`tools/world-art`) | 0 | none beyond `tests/pixel-icons.mjs` |
| 1, sample | 5 icons: Bronze-Rimmed Shield, Kingsgrate, Iron Hatchets, Oak Crossbow, Crown Shard | free route first: the pixel scripts or `pixelart_workbench` (32 supply, drink and kit icons, 11 imbuement items and the Longbow were all drawn this way, 0 credits). If PixelLab: `create_image_pro_flash` 32x32 on `hr-world-56-v2` with a shipped icon as `style_image` (the Glade's six) | 0 free; PixelLab: docs say 10-25 gens per call, per-icon cost not recorded in the repo | Tyler reads the sample at x2 beside the old twins |
| 2 | the other 12 icons outside the Warrens | same | same | one read, then `KNOWN_TWINS` shrinks |
| 3 | the 7 Warrens icons | same | same | with batch 2 or after |
| 4, sample | 2 creatures: Drain Rat, Granary Rat (the most-met repeat) | `create_object_pro_flash`, 8 directions, high top-down, `style_images` of the shipped rat; clips by `animate_object` v3 (`docs/PIXELLAB_WORKFLOW.md` section 1), or the free `tools/world-art/derive-combat.mjs` stand-in clips the Cellar's nine use | the docs' estimate is 5-10 gens a creature east-only (150-300 for ~30); the Glade's five used 8-facing walk and attack plus 4-facing death, which the repo never costed. **Assumed 10-30 gens a creature, 20-60 for the sample, 60-180 for all six.** Free pre-check: `get_balance` and `get_pro_flash_capabilities` | the world-assets guard, a side-by-side with the live rat, Tyler's word per spend |
| 5 | 4 creatures: Sump Slime, Barn Wasp, Barn Spider (or Cistern), Turncoat (a character, `create_character` v3 with a reference) | as 4 | as 4 | as 4 |

**Done:** the audit and the numbers above, the smoke wording, `ASSET_MANIFEST.md` (the live-versus-retired table). **Measured:** every count here, by script over the repo and by walking the local world. **Assumed:** the creature credit range (not in the repo); that a first-20-hours hero stops short of Level 32 (the 10-25 curve puts Level 25 at about 36 hours). **Could still be wrong:** a pixel icon can name the right item and still read badly at x1; I judged the 326 reachable at x4, not at the bag's 1x.

### 2026-11-04 Weapon rungs and the Thicket (designer, lane/c-weapon-thicket): BUILT, measured on the real engine, no migration, the edge deploys. **One choice for Tyler below**
**Weapons (balance 10-25 §8: +5-8% per meaningful upgrade).** The gap is neither clubs alone nor a whole class: it's the box-sweepers. Cleave and spells roll their own numbers and read no weapon power, so a rung reaches a hammer, hatchets or a wand through basic blows only. **Moved: Iron Warhammer 7/12 → 7/16, Steel Warhammer 9/19 → 9/23, Iron Hatchets 7/7 → 9/11, Steel Hatchets 12/11 → 14/14, Klappa's Club 3/12 → 3/16, so it still hits as hard as the Iron Warhammer.** Prices, sources and recipes don't move. Level XP an hour, one tier up, all else equal (`tools/balance-sim` cell runner, the Level's kit, 1 h x 2 seeds, same dice, 0 deaths):
| Rung | Glade 16, as built | Glade 16, pull 8 | Wickstead 15, pull 8 | Warrens 45, as built | Warrens 45, pull 8 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Practice Club → Iron Warhammer | +4.4 → **+5.7%** | +4.6 → **+8.5%** | +3.5 → **+4.7%** | | |
| Iron → Steel Warhammer | | | | +8.3 → **+8.5%** | +7.8 → **+6.5%** |
| Practice → Iron Hatchets | +1.0 → **+5.4%** | +2.4 → **+7.4%** | +4.3 → **+6.3%** | | |
| Iron → Steel Hatchets | | | | +6.0 → **+5.4%** | +7.4 → **+6.2%** |
| Not moved: Oak Wand + book | +4.3% | +5.1% | +1.7% | | |
| Not moved: Willow Wand + book | | | | +5.2% | |
| Not moved: Iron Sword (shield), Longbow, Oak Crossbow, Oak Staff | +8.5, +6.3, +5.3, +11.5% | +10.0, +7.9, +6.2, +12.0% | | | |
| Not moved: Steel Sword (shield), Willow Longbow | | | | +6.5, +4.4% | |
Klappa's Club stays between: over the Practice Club +2.7% (Thicket 5, pull 8), +3.3% (Thicket 10), +4.3% (Wickstead 15), +5.3 and +6.2% (Glade 16); the Iron Warhammer beats it on every one (+4.7, +4.6, +4.7, +5.7, +8.5%).

**What no row can do:** on the Thicket and Wickstead as built the walk sets the pace, not the kill. A scratch 200/200 hammer moves Level XP +3.6% at Thicket 5 and +1.1% at Wickstead 15, and the real rungs +0-0.4%. Only harder monsters would change that, and that moves the Level curve. The wand stays at the floor rather than trading tiers: Oak 7/9 makes Glade +5.2% and takes Willow to +4.2%.

**The Thicket's same-weapon fours (Tyler 11-02: lose about 6-15%; party gold stays below solo).** The pull can't close this. **Moved: only the one-weapon four's table cell, 970 → 995, Level XP only.** Pull stays `[0,4,5,6]`. Per head against the same heroes alone, Level 5 / Level 10, 1 h x 2 seeds, 0 deaths:
| Four of | Before | **Now (995)** | Cell 1000, not built | Pull 7, not built |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Shields | 0.81 / 0.80 | **0.83 / 0.81** | 0.88 / 0.86 | 0.89 / 0.87 |
| Hammers | 0.83 / 0.84 | **0.85 / 0.86** | 0.89 / 0.91 | 0.92 / 0.93 |
| Bows | 0.85 / 0.85 | **0.87 / 0.87** | 0.91 / 0.92 | 0.95 / 0.95 |
| Staffs | 0.82 / 0.83 | **0.84 / 0.84** | 0.88 / 0.89 | 0.88 / 0.89 |
| Wands | 0.97 / 0.97 | **0.99 / 0.99** | **1.04 / 1.05** | **1.14 / 1.15** |
| Mixed four, XP | 1.20 / 1.21 | **1.20 / 1.21** | 1.20 / 1.21 | 1.30 / 1.31 |
| Mixed four, coin | 0.96 / 0.98 | **0.96 / 0.98** | 0.96 / 0.98 | **1.04 / 1.05** |
| Wand four, coin | 1.08 / 1.10 | **1.08 / 1.10** | 1.08 / 1.10 | **1.26 / 1.28** |
Twos and threes were already in the band (worst, shields: 0.85, 0.87) and don't move. **Pull 7 is blocked twice:** mixed coin over solo (11-02) and wand fours over solo (10-31). **Cell 1000 is blocked once:** the share floors per kill (`xp-share.js`), so 995 to 1000 jumps a whelp from 6 to 7 and a wolf from 12 to 13, about +6%, and wand fours read 1.04-1.05 (`party-class-xp-line`'s own cut: 1.016-1.018, over its bar of 1.00; at 995, 0.964-0.966). Nothing lies between 995 and 1000.

**For Tyler: cell 1000?** It lands shields, hammers, bows and staffs inside 6-15% with no gold moved, and it pays a wand-only four in the Thicket 4-5% over solo, against "a same-class party never out-earns solo". Designer's pick: yes, wand-only fours are rare and already over on coin; it needs his word and a named exception in the guard, so it isn't built. **As shipped, shields still lose 17-19%** and staffs 16; hammers and bows are in the band, and wands lose 1%. All of this reads against the same heroes alone, as the 11-02 table did: against a mixed four it can't hold, since a mixed four sits at 1.20 by ruling and one class can't pass 1.00.

**Done:** rows, guards (`weapon-rungs`, three mutants; SG-7 holds the club's words). **Measured:** the tables above. **Assumed:** that real heroes' skills sit near their Level, as the sim's do. **Could still be wrong:** two seeds an hour read about ±1 point (the 11-03 12 h cells landed within 0.7), and the Iron Warhammer at Wickstead's pull 8 is a little under the bar. The hammer and hatchets already led their grounds and now lead by a little more, and role parity isn't ruled. `level-pace.json` wasn't re-measured; a rung only moves XP from Level 15 up, on these two lines. Tier 4 and up have no source, so Mithril's 14/28 is the next step to re-check when it gets one.

### 2026-11-03 Gold matters (designer, lane/c-gold-matters): RULED, measured on the real engine, three files staged, the edge deploys with them
**The ruling.** Gold's first job is the next hero, so the slots carry the first 20 hours: **50,000 / 200,000 / 800,000** (was 15,000 / 40,000 / 120,000), and the refill keeps asking after that: **1,000 + 250 a Level** (was 400 + 100). Coin can't do this work: the profit floors, the starter grounds' ruling, the roster's gold bands and Tyler's new line (harder monsters pay more, nothing rewards staying on a weak ground) leave it about 10% of room in the middle, so coin and sale prices only fix the ladder: **Wickstead about x0.89, the Glade's poachers (the ground x0.94), the Sewer's bat and spider x0.83 with its King x1.04 (a party x0.94, a lone hero x0.89), the Warrens x1.09, tier 2's helmet, body, legs and boots sell at 0.3 of book (was 0.5)**. The Cellar, the Thicket, tier 1 and tier 3 don't move. The gold card comes when the purse is a quarter of the next hero, not all of it, or it would have moved from hour 6 to hour 15.

**Before and after, one lone hero from Level 1, everything sold, real engine** (`tools/balance-sim` plan `gold1103`, seed 0, the six styles' range). Levels don't move: same dice.
| Hour | Level | Earned today | Earned now |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 5-6 | 1,889-2,869 | the same to the gold |
| 3 | 7-8 | 6,746-10,142 | 6,746-9,576 |
| 5 | 8-9 | 12,703-18,748 | 12,010-16,848 |
| 10 | 10-11 | 29,088-39,975 | 26,437-34,998 |
| 15 | 12-14 | 48,127-64,229 | 43,011-55,681 |
| 20 | 14-16 | 64,447-88,098 | 57,661-76,087 |
| 30 | 19-23 | 111,533-151,475 | 98,366-130,156 |
Two heroes (the free two, tank and healer, the Sewer): 158,109 → 146,912 by hour 20. A four: 334,916 → 310,725.

**Walk-through, a new account's purse against real rows** (grepped; played on the local world: gloves 500 → 340 g, then the Homestead refused at 340 g; third hero refused at 49,999 g with `short_by` 1, sold at 50,000 g, next offer 200,000; Bronze, Iron, Steel Platebody paid 90, 120, 210 g; refill quotes 1,250 / 4,250 / 7,250 / 12,250 g at Level 1 / 13 / 25 / 45).
| Hour | Can buy by then (gold; house rows also ask their materials) | Must choose between | Out of reach |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Leather Gloves 160, Bronze Belt 220, Bronze-Rimmed Shield 140, the axe and pick 120, Homestead 400, Millrace Crate 1,500 | Gear or the first house: 500 g start, 1,900-2,900 earned | Nothing |
| 3 | Farmstead 2,500, Thicketwood Chest 6,000, Garden and Cellar first rungs | All of it is about 11,900 g against 6,700-9,600 | Nothing |
| 5 | Stonecross Manor 10,000 | Spend or start saving: What next and the gold card now name the hero | **The third hero: 50,000 g, 9-11 h off. The first real wait; today this purse already holds its 15,000** |
| 10 | Ironbanded Strongbox 25,000, third rungs at 9,000-13,000 | Manor and Strongbox now, or the hero about 5 h sooner | The third hero |
| 15 | The third hero, only if nothing else was bought; Ironvale Keep or the Kitchen at 40,000 | One big step | The fourth hero, 200,000 |
| 20 | The hero and 8,000-26,000 more | Which rooms; a refill at 4,500-5,000 against about 14,000 g that 4 h earn | Fourth hero, Vault 90,000, Forge 75,000 |
A lone hero who buys only heroes pays for the third at hour 14-17, the fourth at about hour 52-62 and the fifth around hour 200 (today hours 4-5, 13-16, 35-42; past hour 30 extrapolated at the lives' last rate, not played). Two heroes reach the third at about hour 8 and the fourth at about hour 25 (today hours 3 and 7; a third hero's income assumed 1.5x two). All of it, bought once, is now about 3.6M, not 2.73M: 210 h of a four, not 150 h. After that the purse buys pace: a bigger pull (Glade L24 at 8 each: hammer's bill 10,958 g/h, XP x1.7) and refills (Level 45, six in a day: 98,000 g against 137,000-209,000 g earned). Neither runs out.

**The first ten minutes didn't move.** Cellar and Thicket coin and tier 1 sales are untouched, so hour 1 is identical on all six styles. Coin pays the bill on every ground a lone hero is sent to at its floor, every style (the worst is the mage at Wickstead L8: about 2,100 g/h of coin, 813 g/h of bill), so a hunt there has no way to end gold_out on the default bar. The one place a bill beats coin in band is a lone hero at the Warrens' floor, a party ground that says "alone from 45", as today.

**Harder pays more, measured** (`forest-cave` `harder-pays-more`, new, three mutants; expected gold, so a lucky hour reads the same). Same hero, one Level, all sold, expected g/h:
| | Thicket | Wickstead | Glade | Sewer | Warrens |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L13 tank / hammer / bow / mage | 2,651 / 3,280 / 3,060 / 3,013 | 2,954 / 3,658 / 3,321 / 3,169 | | | |
| L18 | 2,917 / 3,404 / 3,292 / 3,323 | 3,307 / 3,843 / 3,713 / 3,691 | 3,394 / 4,935 / 4,813 / 5,259 | | |
| L25 | | 3,491 / 3,856 / 3,713 / 3,714 | 4,194 / 5,482 / 5,187 / 5,803 | 5,099 / 7,278 / 5,749 / 6,773 | |
| L45 | | | 5,066 / 5,915 / 5,980 / 6,151 | 6,258 / 7,458 / 6,728 / 7,481 | 5,832 / 8,294 / 5,689 / 8,718 |
A Steel Platebody now sells for 210 g against Iron's 120 g (was 200). **Two known exceptions, both a lone hero:** the Sewer stays over the Warrens at L45 for a tank and a bow (it stays the party ground the game doesn't send anyone to alone, and pays about 40% of the XP), and a bow's shot bill puts the Warrens 5% under the Glade at L45. The party floors stop a deeper Sewer cut. Merged with lane/c-risk-ladder: every number in this section is in band and read the same to the digit on its engine; a rusher under a floor pays its bills, and none of those are quoted here. This answers its flag only in part: the Glade and the Warrens now climb over the ground below at one Level, and the Sewer alone doesn't.

**Rejected.** *Coin x0.5 to chase +25-35%:* that is a minimum, not a cap (10-20), and it can't be reached: the Sewer's party falls under the Thicket at about x0.93 and the Glade in helm and body goes under its bill at about x0.5. *Widening the roster's gold bands to cut deeper:* it would be loosening a guard to get a number; every row here sits inside its band. *Dearer food:* the Glade tank's floor has about 20% of room and the Sewer's crossbow none (+25%, the rule's own edge); the bill is near zero mid-band, so it hits new heroes and misses the rich. *Dearer Shop gear:* the whole Shop is 1,540 g; x3 is 90 minutes, and it hits the 500 g start. *Dearer deeds, chests, rooms:* the early wants that make hours 1-10 a choice, and the bench rooms moved on 11-01. *Blessings:* 1,900 g spent in 14 days, free under 30, a death every 50 h: no price moves that (look again if lane/c-risk-ladder raises deaths). *Tier 2 at 0.3 for everything:* forged weapons sell under their bars (`W-FORGE` red), and the belts and gloves under the outfitter's.

**What could still be wrong.** Real players press the pull: live coin is 4,367 g/h against the sim's about 3,000, so every hour above may come about 1.4x sooner. Live supply stays about 10% of coin: on the mix real players hunt the coin barely moves, because the Warrens rose as Wickstead and the Glade fell (about +930% over the bill, not +940%). The profit rule was never the lever. The Sewer crossbow's floor sits exactly on +25%, so the next coin or bill move there trips `ranger-floor`. Refills have sat at zero since 10-23, with every hero premium and 12 h free, so this price may simply go unpaid. 200,000 for the fourth may discourage four-hero play: watch who stops at three. Nothing here sinks gold past about 210 h of a four: the bank ladder (38.8M, no client path) is the next lever, and that takes a client change. **Done:** rows, guards, staged files. **Measured:** the tables above. **Assumed:** that a wait of 10 h on a hero reads as a goal, not a wall; only tomorrow's players can say.
### 2026-11-03 Rush if you dare: harder ground, more XP, real risk, never blocked (systems-engineer, lane/c-risk-ladder): BUILT, played on the local world, two files STAGED, engine deploy
**Tyler, 2026-11-04 UTC:** "dying should come if people take big risks", "if someone wants to try and 'rush' exp then they should be able to try harder content to get there faster, but it comes with risk", "it's not that complicated". Mechanic only: no other game's names or text.

**What moved.** (1) The 15-Level hard block is gone, for a hero and a party (`hr_hunt_gate`, `belowRangeRefuse`). (2) `underFloorAtkPm` 50 -> 200: a monster's atk x (1 + 0.2 per Level under the floor), accuracy and blow. In range nothing moves: those rows match today's run to the digit. 150 left the Glade's average hero alive at 6 under; 200 kills it in 13 min and a built hero still holds. (3) The warning on the ground's page, one confirm press ("Enter anyway", "Start anyway"), and a why on the death card. (4) **What the block was protecting:** a Level 1 behind three Level 37s in the Warrens stood at full HP, took no blow and earned 190k XP/h; today's block lets a Level 18 do the same. Closed through the share, not a block: a seat under BOTH the ground's floor and two thirds of its party's top living Level keeps (its Level / that) squared of its share (`underFloorSharePm`). Level 1: 39 XP/h. Level 8: 19k. Level 18: 100k. Level 25: full. A party that pushes together, or a low friend on a ground in its range, is paid in full. The party's page says it per seat.

**Does the pay climb?** Yes, at every Level, and nothing cuts a pusher: no XP or drop rule reads a Level but the share above. The same built hero, Level XP an hour: Cellar, Thicket, Wickstead, Sewer, Glade, Warrens.

The build decides the rung: at one Level and ground, poor is minutes, average a gamble, built the best XP in reach.

**Known limits.** The first grounds stay soft under their floors: their blows are so small that a Level 2 in the Thicket or Wickstead holds at 85-90% for the same XP as a floor hero, three times the Cellar's. Left: it is the first hour, and there is little to fear there. BUILT at Level 1 in the Glade holds because the sim hands it the crafted food and all six tier 1 pieces a real Level 1 won't own. A Level 18 behind Level 37s still earns 100k at no risk, about twice the Glade: friends carrying a friend, costing them a share. Solo Hunt again from the summary asks no second confirm: they've already gone in once. A Level 1's 620% reads oddly on the card; it is true. Pull density has no new words: it keeps "Bigger pulls pay more once your heroes can hold them."

### 2026-11-03 Klappa's Club, Tyler's beginner club for Andy (designer, lane/c-klappa-club): BUILT, played on the local world, four files STAGED, nothing applied
**The item.** Klappa's Club (`klappas_club`, Tier 1, Uncommon): a two-handed hammer worn at Level 5, Melee accuracy +3, Melee damage +12, the shop pays 50 g. "A Practice Club banded in bronze and set with Mill Cellar rat teeth: it hits like a warhammer and aims like a practice club. Klappa broke every club in the drill yard finding this one." The kit's Practice Club is 3/7 and the Iron Warhammer 7/12: it takes the Warhammer's damage ten Levels early and keeps the Practice Club's aim. That is Andy's "busted" and its trade-off, in one line. Name if Tyler wants another: Klappa's Cudgel.

**The recipe.** Reforge Klappa's Club, Smithing 1: Practice Club + 6 Bronze Bar + 3 Small Fang, 4.8 s, 46 Smithing XP, 7.5 g at the public bench. A hero can make it once: the kit holds one Practice Club, and nothing sells another. Smithing 1 is deliberate as well as gentle: Sell all loot keeps only what a recipe the hero can make now spends (bag-model.js `madeInto`), so a higher gate sold the fangs on the first trip home.

**The tribute is a thing anyone can make.** No account check and no grant: Andy crafts his the normal way (CLAUDE.md §1, §2). The sprite is ours (§10), drawn on the Practice Club's own axis with its red wrap, a heavier and darker head, two bronze bands and the rat teeth. `art/review/klappa-club-2026-11-03/`.

**Measured** (real settle, `tools/balance-sim/cell.mjs`: one hero, the Melee and Defense a hammer career has at that Level, Bronze plate from the ladder, 12 h a cell, the same dice across a row, 0 deaths in all of them). Level XP an hour:

**Where it comes in.** Level 5 at 14 minutes in the Mill Cellar (40 seeds, 14-14.2). Small Fangs by then: median 6, p10 4, at least 3 in 39 of 40. 12 Copper Ore is 58 s with no tool, 6 Bronze Bars 25 s, the reforge 4.8 s: about a minute and a half at the bench end of a first return. The hero then wears it until Level 15, 17.6 hours of hunting (`level-pace.json`), the longest stretch a club hero had with nothing to look forward to. The Iron Warhammer stays the next step: Smithing 15, or the Sewer's Kobold Tosher.

**Walk-through, each step from the kit and the steps before it** (names checked against `src/data/*` and read off the live UI on the local world):

The coach: one card row, after the first return's chain and before Build your home. Played: it came up at Level 6 on the second trip home, as Klappa's Club, with the whole way in it, and it rings Smithing. With hands empty it stays; once the club is made it moves to the bag ("Open your bag, press it, then Equip") so nobody hunts bare-handed. Any other weapon never sees it, and it has a Got it. What next names the club by itself below Level 5 (`nextGoal`, no edit).

**Purpose test.** Problem: a club hero's weapon does not change for 17.6 hours, and nothing has taught the bench by then. Decision: small on purpose, a first step down gather, smelt, forge. Plugs into Mining, Smithing, the Cellar's drops, and gives Small Fang its first use. Trade-off: damage now, aim later. Hour 100: it has done its job and sells for 50 g. No new system or screen.

**Economy.** It sells for 50 g against 39 g of inputs at the shop's bids and a 7.5 g fee: 3.5 g a hero, once a hero. `craft-wages` and `style-gear` SG-4 hold.

**Known limits.** (1) Sell all loot now keeps Small Fangs for every hero, not only club heroes: a median of 14 in half an hour of Cellar, 3 g each, and the Sell tab or a loot rule sells them. (2) The bench spends the bag, so a worn weapon comes off by hand, one press, said by the row and the card. The Steelgrate gets the same words. (3) `tools/balance-sim` still plays its hammer on the Practice Club until Level 15 (the ladder is the generated family), which under-reads a hammer by 1-3% in that band.

**Discovery for the balance work, no change made.** Practice Club to Iron Warhammer is +2-5% Level XP an hour, under the ruling's +5-8% for a meaningful upgrade. Cleave and Power Strike deal flat damage and carry 60-70% of it (`dealtBy`), so a weapon's numbers reach only the basic blow.

**Server.** Four generated files, in this order, then the edge, then the client: `2027-02-23-klappa-items-catalogue`, `-bench-inputs`, `-bench-tax-rates`, `-hunt-sessions-catalogue`. World shape does not move. The edge hash does, necessarily: the engine holds the club's numbers and recipe.

### 2026-11-02 Harder monsters: the Glade hits more often, BUILT (designer, lane/b-rulings-1102): MEASURED on the built rows, one engine batch, staged
**Tyler's calls:** A, then "drop the 1.25x attack, keep Glade accuracy 0.80 at today's attack". A player door's catch-up stops at its measured engine budget (`hunt-settle.js settleHunt`, 800 ms of wall clock): on GitHub five door calls landed the owed slices, on a loaded PC one 2-min slice a call, so the arm's five cuts stopped at swing 247 of 300 and it read "ended null". A real door is the hunt re-read every 16 s, and the tick takes the rest. The arm now pins the door to the busy case (`engineBudget(0)`) and calls it as often as that takes, so its answer no longer depends on the machine.

### 2026-11-03 A bar slot naming ammo the hero can't use yet (designer, lane/b-rulings-1102): BUILT, played on the local world
Supplies keep their skill (Tyler 10-24); the equip gate held that for the ammo slot and a bar slot walked round it, so a Ranged 42 hero shot and bought Mithril (Ranged 45). **Ruled: the slot stays and waits, never refused.** The engine passes over it, carried or from the store (`abilities.js shotLine`, the one resolver swing and shot spell share), the next slot fires, and it wakes at the level with nothing to press (first principle). The bar strikes it with the lock and says "Needs Ranged 45 — you have 42. This slot waits until then, and your next ammo fires."; Configure action says the same, its Ammo tab locks the row and sorts it last; Hunt prep and Before you go never say it flies or offer it as a spare. With nothing else to shoot the hero ends `no_fight`, and Hunt prep warns first. **Measured** (12 min alone in the Glade, real settle): Ranged 42: 208 shots, all Bronze, Precision Shot 77, Mithril spent 0; Ranged 45: 292 Mithril; store at 42: 0 bought; store at 45: 281; the same bar woke after 13 shots when Ranged reached 45. Viewed: 14 shots, every commit matching. **Played** (`tools/qa/local-world.mjs`, a fresh hero, practice bow, Barbed Arrows at Ranged 1): row locked, words shown, slot struck, 10 min in the Cellar shot Bronze, bought no Barbed, no page errors.

**The search.** Plans `b1102a` to `b1102e` (what-ifs on the real settle, 2-4 h a cell, seeds 0-1; 12 h hunts on the last candidate): every monster's atk x1.5-3, base accuracy 0.65-0.80, HP x3-5, by ground, floor to top, five ways to fight, Level kit and practice set, as built, 4 and 8 each. **Atk and accuracy do not move XP/h, so no Level curve re-fit**; HP does (-60% at x5).

**What could not all hold (why he was asked).** His 60-80% in band against his other rules:
| Pushed to reach it | Typical lowest HP | What breaks |
|---|---|---|
| Glade floor, atk x2.5 | tank 69%, hammer 72%, **bow 89%, mage 93%** | Tank's bill 7,580 g/h on 4,409 earned: loses gold. Bow and mage die (1-2 in 2 h), healer 12 close calls |
| Thicket, atk x3 | L5 tank 92%, hammer 92%, **others 95-100%** | Bow 4 deaths in 2 h, healer 1 and 27 close calls: the solo healer rule |
| Thicket, any accuracy step everywhere (0.80, atk x1.25) | L5 practice tank 86% | Practice tank 2 deaths, 17 close calls, net about 100 g/h: the first hour |
| Thicket and Wickstead, fights 3-5x longer (HP x5, atk x2, accuracy 0.80) | L10 tank 75%, hammer 84% | XP -60%, gold -60%, bill above income, fresh L5 hero 50-92 deaths in 2 h |
Two causes. A kiter is rarely reached, so its typical stays 90-100% whatever the number, and when it is reached the blow kills: deaths come long before 80%. On the first grounds a pack of 2 dies in one or two swings, so there is nothing to feel without a blow big enough to kill a fresh hero.

**What every rule allows** (accuracy step rather than bigger blows, because blows make the spikes: Glade floor at atk x2 gives bow 6 close calls in 2 h; accuracy 0.80 + atk x1.25 gives 0):
| Where, numbers | Melee typical, floor → top | Light armor | Bill floor (tank) | Deaths, close calls | Pull |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Today, Glade | 94 → 100 | 95-100 | 20 g/h | 0, 0 | 8 each at L24 pays more gold |
| **A: Glade accuracy 0.80, atk x1.25** (recommended) | **83-86 → 95** | 87-97 | 1,790, income 4,499 (+151%) | 0, 0 in 2-4 h a cell | L31: 4 each nets more, 8 each 147k XP at about equal gold. L24: 4 each more XP, less gold. L16 at 8: 1 death, 18 close calls in 2 h, loses gold |
| B: Glade accuracy 0.80, atk x1.5 | **80-82 → 93** | 84-96 | 2,890, income 3,946 (+37%) | 0, 0 as built | 4 each loses gold until about L31; L16 at 8: 40 deaths in 2 h. "Bigger pull pays once held" fails for most of the band |
| Wickstead accuracy 0.65, atk x1.25 | 94 → 98 | 98-100 | 10 | 0; practice hammer 1 close call in 2 h | |
| Cellar, Thicket | unchanged: first hour and solo healer | | | | |
**So 60-80% as built is out of reach everywhere under his other rules.** At the pull, Normal is real: under A, L24 at 4 each 60-71%, L31 at 8 each 59%.

**Also built:** level gap (`underFloorAtkPm` 50), "before bonuses" fix, Thicket `groupFit: both`, 4 seats, pull `[0,4,5,6]`, table; Houndskin Cloak on the Glade Poacher; Wickstead Rares wear at 15; Tamsin's Glade task pays Steel; measured party line (`PARTY_VS_ALONE`); per-party drop words; pull card. **`xp-share.js:59` floor: left alone.** It costs 5-10% only on Cellar shares, the curve is fitted over it, and fixing it moves Level 1-5 pace for nothing a player reads. **Proposal 1, re-ruled:** build the words now, a card at Level 10 naming + and its price, since harder monsters make a big pull a real risk. The "biggest pull held" readout waits for real picks.

**Tyler's party call, measured at the new Thicket row** (pull `[0,4,5,6]`, table `[980,1290] [970,1180,1310] [970,1210,1260,1300]`; plan `b1102t`, 1 h x 2 seeds, Level kit, per head against the same heroes alone, Level 5 / Level 10):

One table row can't close a 20-point gap between weapons. The Party window says "about 10%" for two and three of a class, "about 15%" for four. **Party coin per head:** mixed 0.90-0.98, every same-weapon party but wands 0.88-0.99: below solo. **Wand-only parties are 1.05-1.12 on coin** (net 0.89-1.12, their bill up 27-43%), breaking "party gold below solo" in that one case. **Why it stands:** the only lever is the pull, and one step narrower (`[0,4,4,5]`, plan `b1102u`, L10) takes wands to 0.86-0.95 coin but drops the mixed three to 1.00 XP, losing its bonus, and same-weapon friends to 0.71-0.87. **The chase already exists and nobody can see it: how big a pull you can hold.** At L16 in the Glade, 8 each loses gold (tank net 4,294 → 2,730; 58% typical); at L24 it pays (4,461 → 6,518; 79%). Armor moves that line: Iron against Bronze at 8 each is net x4.6 to x7. So Level, gear, food and style all feed one number the player chooses, and it answers "why does this piece matter". It is on one stepper, with no goal and no readout.

**Tried and rejected, on the same dice:** monster HP x2: XP -19 to -38% as built, net -24 to -47%, typical HP still 95-100% on the first grounds; it lifts the weapon step only to +3-7% and needs a Level curve re-fit. **Hask's Ford: STAYS ON HOLD for the invite; first content lane after it.** Its band is 45-60, so it answers nothing a veteran quits over at hour 22-30, and the branch is 12 releases behind with its own Level curve re-fit. It should come off when the first beta hero passes Level 35, or at the end of beta week one: a lone hero has no new ground from 32 to 45, a pressed hammer is Level 32 at hour 28.6, and a two-week beta at 12 h a day is 168 h. The Level 32-45 gap stays open either way.

**Rooms, measured.** Kitchen rung 1's 40,000 g is hour 11-12 of hunting, spending nothing (the 11-01 table said 18 h; careers sell all their loot). Tax saved doing both halves, played: Herring 248, Trout 337, Iron 270 (Forge owned: 1,530 → 1,800 g/h, confirmed), Oak 289, Willow 371, Hatchets 575 g/h. At 12 h a day: Kitchen 10-13 days, Forge 11-23, Workshop 17-22, Library about 9 (its both-halves rate computed, not played). **Ruling: keep every first rung.** It's what Tyler asked for, and rooms are the long goal for someone who crafts. Garden stays reachable: 20 Wheat is 30% off the Cellar's Giant Rat. A hunter should never want a Kitchen, and that's fine; the fix is the words: Deed I's "Opens the Garden, the Kitchen" reads as a mis-priced reward, so say what each costs and who it's for. **The whole visible ladder is about 2.8M g:** one hero at the Glade 36-47 days of 12 h, a four-hero account 9-12 days. No recurring sink exists until Level 30 Blessings beyond food and stamina refills (at L25, 2,900 g for 4 h: good value at 5-6k g/h, excluded from the baseline). Imbuing is the sink and gear chase the game promises and hasn't built (`imbuements.js`): after the invite, and not a tuning job. **Watch four-hero purses in the beta.**

**The friends' party test (blind, v0.2.82), confound removed.** Mill Cellar, 3 seeds of 1 h, three heroes each, L3 and L6:
| | Level XP/h a hero | Gold net |
|---|---|---|
| Mixed three (tank, bow, healer), default pull 5 | 8,327-8,505 (his 8,164-8,356) | 1,779-1,876 |
| One class, same pull, same kills | 6,645-6,675 | 1,808-1,840 |

| Same heroes alone at 5 each | 9,525-12,978 | 2,509-3,274 |
(1) **The bonus is real and meets the ruling:** the mean against those heroes alone is 1.17-1.20 (§5 trio 1.10-1.20). His +11%, +14% and +37% are one even share against three different lone speeds. Mixed against one class reads 1.25-1.27, not 1.18, because each kill's share floors to a whole number (`xp-share.js:59`) and costs more on a smaller share: about 0.5 XP on 7-9. Tutorial-room scale; flagged for the engine batch. **Gold unchanged: true, as ruled.** A lone hero pressing to the same 5 out-earns the party by about 40%, the pressed gap again. **The promise misleads, and worse elsewhere:** "18% more Level XP" is the table's factor on the share, not more than alone. Thicket reads "52% more" for a measured 1.19; Wickstead 27% for 1.20; the Glade 32% for 1.19. **Ruled words:** "Your party has 3 of 4 classes: about 20% more Level XP each than hunting alone here." One class: "One class only: about 15% less Level XP each than alone here." From a measured `vsAlone` row, played by the guard. His P2-7 is **a bug**: `accrual.js:1401` builds `raw_xp` from the tally's paid XP, so "before bonuses" always equals the total; the `xp` event's `raw` never reaches it. Engine batch, or hide the line until then.
(2) **Per kill for the whole party, not per hero.** Each kill rolls once at the party's yield (0.85 for three, 0.55 alone) and each drop goes to one hero (`hunt-rewards.js:91,104`). Per hero per hour: 313 kills x 0.85 against 468 x 0.55 = about 1.03x. Played: 0.94-0.99x gold. **Ruled words:** "chance per kill, for your party of 3: each drop goes to one of you", and below it, "A party's kills drop more often and the drops are shared, so each hero's loot per hour stays about the same as alone." A Rare says the same: the roll picks one.

**Walk-through for proposal 3** (grepped: `hunt-grounds.js`, `hunts.js`, `hunt-list.js recommendation`; L7 four played 32 h; the row change itself not played)
| Step | Holds | Doable because |
|---|---|---|
| L1, party forms, Hunts recommends the Cellar | Practice sets | band 1-8, groupFit both |
| L5, Hunts recommends the Thicket (today: Cellar) | Same, plus Cellar drops | band 5-15 holds Level 5, highest floor that fits once it's `both` |
| L8, Hunts recommends the Sewer | Thicket's armor drops | floor 8 beats 5; the tutorial's "Into the sewer, together" is unmoved |
| Lone hero | | `solo` also fits `both`: path unchanged, Level curve untouched |
Run `hunt-floor-warning`, `first-week-bands`, `forest-cave` on the row. The Thicket has 2 start tiles; a four spawned fine in the settle, but `hunt-spawn.js:97` refuses more heroes than tiles on one path, so give it 4.

**For Tyler: three choices.**
1. **Danger and big pulls.** As built, nothing threatens a geared hero (87-100% HP). Pressing + is where danger and cost live, and it doubles the pace (Level 25 in 17 h against your 36). **A (recommended): keep it. The default stays safe for 12 h away, the pull is the danger dial, tell players so, and read the beta's real picks.** B: harder monsters (HP x2, blows x1.5): first grounds still 92-96%, 19-38% less XP, 25-67% less gold, curve re-fit. C: bigger pulls pay less per monster: an engine change, and it dulls the one decision Level 10-30 has.
2. **Parties that earn less.** Mixed parties now hit your XP targets as built. Same-weapon friends earn 5-17% less, and 16-31% less in the Thicket. Mixed parties net 0.70-1.03x gold, which you haven't ruled (§13). **A (recommended): widen the Thicket's pull so same-weapon friends lose 6-15%, and leave party gold below solo.** B: change nothing. C: pay party gold up to solo: a new faucet.
3. **How far under a floor?** Today 15 Levels, so Level 7 friends enter the Glade for 2.3x XP and 1.9x gold. The gap term makes it cost a lone hero; a party with a healer shrugs it off. **A (recommended): refuse from 6 under (`belowRangeRefuse`), so Glade from 10, plus the gap term.** B: keep 15, gap term only, and let parties push as a party's reward. C: refuse from 3: safest and least fun.

**Handoffs.** Warrens Enter lit at L13 though the server refuses (veteran P2; 19 under). Party trap: a weak alt's death leaves the strong hero in a party with a dark Enter (P1 by his steps, not reproduced here). Bag at 114 of 100 with no warning. All Coordinator triage.

**Done / measured / assumed.** Done: tools and tables, this section; no game data moved. Measured: every number above except as named. Assumed: the mixed rows in proposal 6, pay-backs at 12 h a day, ladder days at 5-6.5k g/h, and that a shop sale prices loot (a real market is unmeasured).

### 2026-11-01 The bench tax and what a house bench costs (designer, lane/b-bench-prices; Tyler: "make building workbenches in your house WAY more expensive"): ROOM PRICES PLAYED AND STAGED, TAX COMPUTED until lane/c-bench-tax lands
**The ruling.** A public bench charges 15% of what the job's output sells for at the shop, rounded to the nearest gold, in every bench skill: Smithing, Crafting, Cooking, Runecrafting. The Great Hearth is not a bench and stays free. Cheap goods round to nothing, so a fresh hero pays nothing: Copper Bar (3 g), Normal Plank (3 g), Cooked Shrimp (3 g), and goods with no shop price (Bronze Arrows, Chalked Runes, Coarse Whetstones). No job's fee can exceed what its output sells for, so pressing Start can't lose money on a priced good, and if the purse runs out the stack in the bag pays it back. A house bench is the first rung of its room (Kitchen, Forge, Workshop, Library), shared by the whole account.

**Fee = round(0.15 x shop bid x output count), per job.** Row shape asked of lane/c-bench-tax: `{ smithing: 150, crafting: 150, cooking: 150, runecrafting: 150 }` in permille. If that branch ships a flat gold-per-craft row instead, the nearest honest numbers are 0 below recipe level 15 and 1 g a craft from there, which takes far less from gear and steel; ask me to re-rule.

**After-tax wage, COMPUTED from the real rows and the measured times (not played: the mechanic has not landed).** One hero doing both halves at a public bench, gold/h.

Own food stays far under the store: a Cooked Shrimp costs 1 g of raw fish and no fee against 14 g for 72 HP, a Goldgill 4 + 2 g, a Turnip Juice about 1 g a drink against 10 g. A mage's Chalked Runes are free; a Ranger's Barbed Arrows pay 8 g for 50, under 1 g a shot at the store.

"Time after" is hours hunted at the 10-31 pace on the board (40,925 g at 18.1 h, 64,450 g at 27.6 h, spending nothing else). Rungs 2 to 4 climb to 110k / 160k / 240k (Forge, Workshop), 70k / 110k / 180k (Kitchen), 90k / 130k / 200k (Library), with materials; they buy a little speed, so they are a sink for the rich. Held rung 5s rise above them. A hero benching 2 h a day from stock on Iron saves 1,500 g a day: 50 days.

**Risks, each with my recommendation: ship.** (1) **The house is account-shared, so four crafting heroes pay it back in 3 to 5 days.** That is the fastest case and it takes four heroes working all day; I would not price for it. (2) The Kitchen was a liked first purchase (panel, hour 3). The home stays quiet longer; Garden (600 g) and Cellar (1,200 g) stay cheap and keep the early goal. (3) A hero who already bought a cheap room keeps it free of tax until the wipe. (4) The hour figures are one played hero's gold; time to afford is not a sim. (5) A purse at 0 can't start a taxed job: lane/c-bench-tax must say why and name the stack to sell. Gathering is free, so nobody is stuck.

**Ladder checks.** Deeds, Garden, Cellar, Shrine and Trophy Room are byte-equal in the generated rows. The Bear Pelt goal is untouched: 5 for the Keep, 2 and 4 for the Trophy Room, Brook Bear at the Glade. A deed now costs less than the room it opens (2,500 g opens a 75,000 g Forge), and that is fine: a deed also pays plots and opens the Cellar. Every new material has a door (catalogue-coherence C12 green). Played on the local world: each deed and first room refused one gold short (`insufficient_gold`) and sold at its price.

### 2026-11-01 What a skill pays at the shop (designer, lane/c-craft-economy): MEASURED on the real settle and sale, RULED, STAGED. **Tyler should see this one; it ships unless he overrules**
**The ruling.** The shop is a floor, not a wage. Per hero-hour at the hours played: sold raw about half of hunting, gather and refine about two thirds, the whole chain to a finished piece about four fifths, and never above hunting, because a resting hero skills for free (stamina refills 1:1 outside a hunt). The better price for a skiller is the Weighhouse, where a floor of 6 g on an Iron Bar now leaves room to sell to someone who wants to skip the labor. PROVISIONAL until an economy sim says otherwise. Hunting's own numbers did not move on the first five grounds.

**Method.** `tools/qa/craft-economy.mjs` on the local world: two accounts, `set_activity` starts a row, town time passes, `set_activity idle` collects, `vendor_sell_many` sells; what the purse moved is the answer. Every node and recipe, 2 h, 2 samples per row (identical on every fixed-yield row; burn and 1-2 a swing rows within 3%). Before: 71049c02e. After: this branch. "Both halves" is one hero's whole cycle, composed from the measured rows; six were then played as whole cycles on the fixed rows and matched within 1%, Herring 10% lower (more burn at Cooking 8 than over a 2 h run).

**Skill levels did not move** (no XP, no gather or bench time changed). Mining 15 / 30 / 45 / 60 / 75 at 1 / 2 / 8 / 29 / 114 h of that one job, Woodcutting 1 / 3 / 11 / 37, Fishing 1 / 4 / 9 / 31 / 113. A closed browser and an open one are the same rate: Iron Rock 8 h away 4,000 ore, 8 h in 10 min windows 4,000, Mining 26 to 46 either way (the panel's 26 to 47 was real). A window caps at 12 h: 24 h away pays 6,000. Gold along the way: Mining to 60 by 29 h 198,839 → 40,217 g; Woodcutting to 60 by 37 h 254,658 → 55,228; Fishing to 60 by 31 h 195,035 → 44,653. A hunter at 28 h holds 64,450 (board, 10-31).

**The largest faucet, and where gold goes.** Gold from nothing at the shop was refined goods by a wide margin: `vendor_sell` of bars, planks and cooked fish at their whole book, while raw goods pay a fifth, so one smelt was a 7x to 18x raise. A 12 h hunt plus 12 h of rest at the bench (Wickstead, Iron Bars): hunting 42.7k, skilling 324k, or 88% of the day. After: 42.7k and 21.6k, 34%. Sold raw: 41% before, 22% after. Replaying the panel crafter's sales: 97,937 g → 10,156 g, plus 15,200 g hunting and 11,675 g of stock left unsold, against the 41,500 g he spent. By that count (mine, not played) he can just about cover it at hour 27 by selling everything, so money still matters. Sinks are unchanged and sized for hunting: hero slots 15k / 40k / 120k, chests 1.5k to 90k, deeds 400 to 40k, rooms, stamina refills, Blessings past 30, the store bill, the market's 1% fee and 4% cut.

**How it is built: data rows only.** `src/data/items.js` adds BULK_GOODS (bars, planks, Blank Runes, Timber Beams, Iron Fittings, Keystones, tools) to the raw list, so the shop pays a fifth of book; their book stays what the market's ceiling, SG-4 and the tools' shop price read. Book values fall on the gathered and bulk rows, the cooked fish, the whetstones and the runes (`RUNE_VALUE`). Recipes: Iron Hatchets 6 bars 2 planks, Warhammer 6 + 3, Ironbound 9 + 3, Bronze Gauntlets 3 bars, Barbed and Iron Bolts 3 + 3, Iron Arrows 1 + 6, Mithril Arrows 2 + 2, Cut Blank Runes 6 essence, Scored binds 18 blanks. Guard: `tests/craft-wages.mjs` (W-RAW, W-CHAIN, W-STEP, W-FORGE, W-ROOM, W-USE; 15 of 15 mutants caught), every row to level 99.

**Forged gear's reason.** Two now. A price: every piece a gatherer can make from rock and tree sells above the shop's bid for its metal, and forging pays more than selling the bars (hatchets 2,463 against bars 1,800). A use: it is the weapon rung of its style now, where the drop is a 0.37% Rare on the Sewer's Kobold Tosher. Walk-through below. The two tier-3 pieces (Coppercap, Steelgrate) sell at 0.14 of book by the 10-23 ruling, so they are made to be carried, and the guard names them. **Drops selling for the same as forged is separate and stays:** one price per item wherever it came from (10-23). Monster drops of these pieces are Rares, so they don't flood the bench's price.

**The Weighhouse.** Floor = the shop's bid, played: one under it refused `price_below_shop`, one at it listed, bought by a second account, seller paid. Before, the floor killed trades: Cooked Herring heals 72 and could not list under 40 g while the store sells 72 HP for 14 g. Now its floor is 5, so a cook can undercut the store (guard W-ROOM). The suggested chip still reads book, 30 g on an Iron Bar. At a 1 g floor a single unit pays the seller 0 after the 4% cut, so 1 g goods sell in lots. Cooked Shrimp still can't list at all: it is in the start kit (`start_kit`, threat model #4). Not moved here; a cook will notice.

**Hunt loot, named.** Four goods also drop: Copper Ore, Coal, Iron Ore, Gold Ore. Same engine, same dice, real settle, 2 seeds of 4 h: Cellar, Thicket, Wickstead 0 (no row). Glade L24 -0.2% (4,635 → 4,624; 6,080 → 6,067). Warrens four at L40 -3.4 to -4.0% a hero (5,332 → 5,149, 5,126 → 4,919); Warrens L46 alone -3.8% (7,837 → 7,539). The bills there are 0 to 227 g/h (10-31 verdict), so the +25% minimum stands by a wide margin. Kill bounds, spawns and limits are byte-equal to the party-pull file, and the generated self-check still executes the settle's item bound for every droppable item and refuses a dropped item that lost its bid. No level curve or party XP number moves.

**Self-use goods, words only, no row moves.** Yes: a player should learn before crafting. Bronze Arrows, Coarse Whetstones and the six Chalked Runes stay at no price. At 1 g a rune a Chalked job is 45 g in 22 s, 7,360 g/h, the best money in the game, so the answer to "12,375 runes and no buyer" is a sentence and not a price. lane/a-item-truth's wording, derived from the data so a new row files itself (shop bid 0 on ammo): on the recipe row and card, **"For your own use: the shop won't buy these and the Weighhouse won't list them."** On the tooltip, in place of "The shop has not priced this yet": **"Made for your own use. The shop doesn't buy it and it can't be listed."** No confirm step on the bench.

**Walk-through: a fresh hero's first forged weapon** (played by `--walk`, no fixture past the sign-up, on the fixed rows)
| Hours | Step | Holds from the steps before | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Sign up | 500 g | `hr_create_hero` |
| 0.5 | Copper Rock (Mining 1) | 374 Copper Ore, Mining 16 | rock opens at 1 |
| 1.5 | Copper Bar until Smithing 15 (Smithing 1) | 561 Copper Bars, Smithing 17, Mining 20 | ore from the step before; more gathered between |
| 1.55 | Iron Rock (Mining 15) | 25 Iron Ore | Mining 20 |
| 1.6 | Iron Bar (Smithing 15) | 25 Iron Bars | ore from the step before |

Worn at Level 15, which is Level, not Smithing (10-24). Not played: wearing them.

**For Tyler, each with my recommendation: ship.** (1) Every raw and refined bid falls 2x to 15x: Iron Bar 90 → 6, Oak Plank 55 → 6, Cooked Herring 40 → 5. It's the point of the change; a skiller's shop income falls about 15x to half of hunting. (2) Forged recipes take 2x to 4.5x the bars; the first upgrade for a hatchets, hammer or shield hero adds one to two minutes of work. (3) Warrens loot -3.4 to -4.0% a hero. I would not compensate coin for it. (4) A mage's own runes cost more stone: Cut Blank Runes 6 essence for 12, Scored binds 18 blanks. At the Cellar's 160 runes an hour (board, 10-31) a hero on Scored spends about 4 minutes of work per hunting hour, was about 2; other burn rates are unverified. "Hunting at the same hours" is my judgment on measured rates, not a long-run sim's answer.

**Known limits.** Rows above level 60 are in the wage by the same rule, because a resting second hero reaches Mining 75 in 114 h, inside one beta; nothing above Warrens hunting exists to judge them against, so their anchor (7,000 g/h) is assumed. Farm-limited and hunt-limited goods stayed (Wheat Bread 120, Wolf Meat 12, cloth and oak-line gear): their stock is the hunt's or the plot's, not a hero's hour; a player can't scale them from a rock. Tools with a shop price stay at book for that rule and are bid at a fifth. **Exploit review:** an input got cheaper, so buying at the shop and selling back stays a loss (vendor-shop-arbitrage 0). Stock held at the moment of apply sells at the old price until the edge lands, then the new; nothing can be sold above what it cost after. Between the hunt catalogue and the edge the floor is new and the shop still old, so a listing at the new floor could be bought and sold on for minutes: apply and deploy together, before the beta's accounts join.

### 2026-10-31 Blind panel, round 2: rulings and three items for Tyler (designer, lane/b-panel-rulings): PLAYED on the local world, MEASURED on the real engine at v0.2.78
**Method.** Panel round 2 (`art/review/player-feedback-2026-11-01/`, veteran and newcomer, simulated, time-skipped): evidence, not verdicts. About 210 cells through `tools/balance-sim` cell.mjs (the real settle), 1 to 24 hero-hours each. Store supplies only, so bills are the top of the range: a hero eating its own food pays less (the veteran's 42 gp in 30 min against the sim's 570). Played on `tools/qa/local-world.mjs`: a fresh sword hero through both returns, 1440x900, page closed for each absence. **Not played:** a phone, a party, a real clock.

**1. Level band words: BUILT.** "Expect losses" was false for an armored hero and true for an unarmored one, so it reads now: "This hunt is above your level: more XP, but monsters hit harder. In weak armor you can spend more on food than you earn, or die."
| Level 11 sword + shield, first hour | Level XP/h | Store bill g/h | Net g/h | Deaths |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wickstead (fits), armor | 25.8k | 0 | 3,344 | 0 |
| Glade (above), armor | 47.5k | 1,140 | 2,651 | 0 (54k XP/h, 0 in 24 h over 12 h hunts) |
| Glade (above), practice set | 47.9k | 6,780 | -2,480 | 0, 2 close calls |
| Glade, Level 2 sword, practice set | 44.6k | 16,500 | -12,929 | 4 in 2 h |
| Warrens alone, Level 22, armor (10 under) | 94.6k | 24,500 | -21,059 | 0 |
Every style, in every cell under a floor, earns more XP. Ruling: pushing up pays XP and costs food; that is Tyler's own 10-18 "waste hunt", and it is now a choice the screen states. "Fits your level" stays: it means the ground that holds your Level. `first-week-bands.mjs above-your-level-words` plays each claim on the engine, so a balance change that makes one false goes red.

**2. The return: BUILT, words and existing screens.** What was the harness and what was real:
| Panel saw | Cause | Now |
|---|---|---|
| "While you were away (10 min)" after 200 min; no recap at all | HARNESS: a skip moves the hunt back, not the clock on, so the stamp read real seconds | `world.wait(n, { away: true })` moves it. On a real absence the line fires: "(8 h 3 min) ... 3,825 kills. Still hunting, with 3 h 58 min of stamina left." |
| "Taking a beating?" at 35 meals in 8.5 h, over a full-health hero | REAL | A beating is 3 meals at 30 an hour or more. The "just now" cards stand aside for the hunt the player came back to, and no card opens over it: it starts folded |
| "Your stamina ran out." and nothing else | REAL | End card: why, how it comes back, "Stamina now: 4 h 14 min". Away line: "The hunt has ended 4 h 14 min ago." |
| 5 h 46 m idle with no warning on leaving | REAL | Before you go, every hunt: "Stamina: 3 h 17 min. Your hero hunts that long, even with the game closed, then comes home." |
| "Paid today 2 of 3" never resetting, dates out of order | HARNESS | Left |

**3. Coach load: BUILT.** Cards before hunt 2: 17 to 13; in town between hunts 7 to 5. The first two hunts need: what fights, the Level ring, the farm, the bar, Cash in, Blessings, Sell, the Hearth, Tamsin, the Thicket. The Hearth stays, on evidence: the hero walking across town was the newcomer's best minute, and at 16 Bones it is quick. The spell offer and Help wait for the Thicket, where there is time to read. Cook, Decide once and "Defense isn't training" wait for the second return. **Decide once asks for one thing**, not a form: "Pick one thing you only ever sell and set it to Sell: the game remembers." "More than one hero" gets a Got it: it sat ahead of the Thicket's lessons in every hunt past 5 minutes for anyone who wants one hero.

**4. What to want: RULED, built as words. No new content.**
| Hour | Level, gold (panel, sim) | What to want | Where it says so |
|---|---|---|---|
| 5 | 6-8, 3-6k | The armor set, Wickstead at 8, a third hero | Equip upgrades; What next; "A third hero costs 15,000 gp" from a quarter of the price |
| 12 | 10-11, about 16k | A third hero now, the Weighhouse, the Iron tier at 15 | "You can pay for a third hero (15,000 gp): press Characters." New card at Level 10, its icon ringed |
| 20 | 15-16, 25-40k | Iron on, the Glade at 16, a fourth hero at 40,000, the next house deed, your place in Havora | Equip upgrades; What next; new card at Level 16 for the Roll of Honor, its icon ringed |
Prices are the server's (`account.hero_slot_offer`, the house's own section). Both cards are one read each. **Flagged, not built:** Sell all loot leaves spare armor to one click a stack (newcomer, 47 of 100 spaces), a decision repeated by hand; the first-principle fix is a loot plan for gear below what is worn.

**Walk-through: a fresh sword hero's first two hunts** (the machine check is `sewer.mjs tutorial-walk-through`; M = played)
| Step | Card | Holds and can reach, from the kit and the steps before | Doable because |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Every weapon is a style | Six practice sets, sword and shield worn | Got it (M) |
| 2 | Rats in the mill | Hunts window; Cellar band 1-8 | Enter (M) |
| 3-4 | Your hero fights alone, Your first Level | Hunt running | Server finishes them (M) |

| 7 | Out to the brook, Cash in | Level 5 at about 16 min (M: Level 5 at 18) | Leave hunt (M) |

| 9 | Before the wolves | Free below Level 30 | Shop, Services, Bless (M, card) |

| 14 | Paid in town | Wolves counted while hunting | Leave |
| 15 | Decide once | This hunt tab holds Thicket loot; shop bids on what the bag holds | One row to Sell, or Got it (M, card) |
