**The panel's three balance items.** (b) is decided, by Tyler, below. On (a) and (c), my recommendation to him: change no number for Sunday. All three read as separate problems and have one cause: **as built, the walk between packs sets the pace**, so more power or more bodies have little to do. A respawn cut by party size moved Wickstead's duo from 20.0k to 20.4k XP/h per hero. The pull size is where both show, and it works: the veteran's "+ did nothing" did not reproduce (As built, 3 each, 4 each, 5 each, one press each). So the Hunts window now says "Bigger pulls pay more once your heroes can hold them."

**(a) No danger in band.** In-band armor: typical lowest HP 94-98%. Tyler's Normal is 60-80%, though his ruling calls those ranges diagnostic and the hard targets hold: 0 deaths. Blow size is the wrong lever, because it hurts the wrong hero first.
| Monster blows, Glade Level 16 in armor | Tank: typical / worst tenth, bill g/h | Bow, Healer, Mage: deaths, close calls, bill g/h |
|---|---|---|
| Today | 94 / 83, 27 | 0, 0, 10-462 |
| x1.5 | 91 / 74, 267 | 0 in 8 h each, 0, 165-676 |
| x2 | 88 / 63, 740, net 3,594 from 4,340 | 0 in 8 h each, 3 / 0 / 2, 866-1,261 |
| x3 | 82 / 53, 2,500; 0 deaths in 24 h | **Bow 2, Healer 3, Mage 6 deaths in 4 h**, 21-49 close calls |
Even at x3 the tank is still Easy while the light armors die. Put on every tier, x1.5 also gives a fresh practice-set hero in the Thicket 14 close calls in 6 h, against 0 today. **Recommend:** keep 5.4 and 4.0 for the beta. The danger that exists is the pull size: at 8 each a fresh Thicket hero sits at 58% and a fresh Wickstead hero at 52% with a death in 4 h, while in-band armor sits at 92-97%. That is already a choice a player makes. First candidate after a week of real play: **tiers 2-3 x2 (8.0 for tier 3, and a new 10.8 row for tier 2)**. It touches the Glade, Turncoats, the Warrens and the Thicket's Dire Gray and Old Grayjaw, not the Cellar or the Sewer (all tier 1; the Sewer cells did not move). No death in 8 h a style in the Glade, and a bill that finally gives cooking a consumer. It risks an edge deploy, profit floors nobody has re-measured, a fresh Thicket hero against those two wolves, and a Warrens party unplayed at that number. Even so the typical encounter stays Easy, so Normal will need pack size per ground, not blows.

**(b) Party XP: DECIDED (Tyler, 2026-10-31: "Party exp should only be better if there are different classes in the party"). Supersedes "no party bonus" (10-22); the 10-25 §5 targets apply to mixed parties. Rule below is the designer's, measured; the engine change is its own lane, NOT BUILT here.**

*Baseline today, Level XP per hero against the mean of the same heroes alone, same ground, Level and pull* (v0.2.78, 1 h, 1 or 2 seeds; L5-L31 ranges from the 10-31 verdict at v0.2.74). Mix changes nothing yet: the walk sets the pace, whoever is in the party.
| | Mixed duo | Mixed four | Same class duo | Same class four |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Thicket, Wickstead, Glade, as built | 0.68-0.75 | 0.49-0.59 | 0.64-0.78 | 0.46-0.58 |
| Same grounds, 8 each | 0.83-0.95 | 0.82-0.90 | 0.81-0.96 | 0.69-0.84 |
| Sewer L8-L25, as built | 1.14-1.21 | 1.01-1.10 | **1.13-1.18** | 0.91-1.03 |
| Sewer, 8 each | 1.00-1.12 | 0.92-0.97 | **0.95-1.05** | 0.84-0.98 |
| Warrens L32-L50, 8 each | 1.03-1.05 | 1.13-1.23 | 0.99 (tank; a lone bow stalled at 8 each, 2,141 XP/h) | 0.95 |
Against where a lone hero is sent: the Warrens party already earns about 2x the Glade, and the Sewer duo and four 1.27x and 1.06x at Level 8, 0.73x and 0.65x at Level 17.

*Classes: four, not five.* Melee (shield or not), Ranger, Mage, Healer. Tyler 10-16: "Tank is a function (a Melee with shield/taunt), not a spec", and the screen says Melee on both. Tank + hammer measured no different from two tanks (0.85 and 0.91 at 8 each). A crossbow and shield is a Ranger.

*THE RULE: Level XP only, never gold.* `share = monster XP / living heroes`, then `x table[ground][living heroes][different classes among the living]`, read fresh at every kill. **And a party's default pull grows with its size, per ground; a lone hero's never moves** (2026-11-01: most players never press +, and with the bonus alone a mixed party as built stayed under solo, duo 0.86, four 0.74). Pull and table are both data rows per ground.
| Ground | Default pull, 2 / 3 / 4 heroes | 2 heroes, classes 1 / 2 | 3 heroes, 1 / 2 / 3 | 4 heroes, 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Thicket | 4 / 5 / 6 | 1.00 / **1.15** | 1.00 / 1.13 / **1.27** | 1.00 / 1.13 / 1.20 / **1.27** |
| Wickstead | 3 / 3 / 4 | **0.90** / **1.05** | 1.00 / 1.13 / **1.27** | 1.00 / 1.13 / 1.20 / **1.27** |
| The Glade | 3 / 4 / 4 | 1.00 / **1.18** | 1.00 / 1.13 / **1.27** | 1.00 / 1.13 / 1.20 / **1.27** |
| The Sewer | as built | **0.80** / 1.00 | 0.80 / 1.05 / **1.10** | 0.80 / 1.05 / 1.10 / **1.15** |
| The Warrens | as built | 1.00 all | 1.00 all | 1.00 all |
| Mill Cellar | as built | 1.00 / 1.20 | 1.00 / 1.13 / 1.30 | 1.00 / 1.13 / 1.27 / 1.40 |
**The first table has to change:** 1.20 / 1.30 / 1.40 on top of a grown pull lands 1.33-1.39 for a four. It moves to 1.27, and the duo to a value per ground.

*Why pull alone fails.* At any pull, a mixed party and a same-class party earn about the same per head (Wickstead duo at 3 each: 1.00-1.11 and 1.05-1.10; Glade four at 8 each: 1.18 and 1.12-1.30). A pull that lifts one lifts the other. So each pull above is the largest where same-class stays at or under 1.00 with no cut. The one exception is the Wickstead duo: 3 each is the smallest step up from as built (0.72), and same class is already 1.01-1.10 there, so it takes the 0.90.

*Measured, per head against the same heroes alone AS BUILT* (real settle, v0.2.78; parties 1 h x 2 seeds, lone heroes 1 h x 3; the same-class cells nearest the line played again on fresh seeds). The bonus is applied to the measured ratio.
| Ground, pull | Mixed before bonus | Mixed with it | §5 | Same class, with its row |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Thicket duo, 4 each | 0.87-1.00 | 1.00-1.15 | 1.05-1.15 | 0.88-0.95 |
| Thicket trio, 5 each | 0.89 | 1.13 | 1.10-1.20 | 0.90-0.94 |
| Thicket four, 6 each | 0.95 | 1.21 | 1.15-1.25 | 0.85-0.92 |
| Wickstead duo, 3 each | 1.00-1.11 | 1.05-1.17 | 1.05-1.15 | 0.91-0.99 |
| Wickstead trio, 3 each | 0.92-0.93 | 1.17-1.18 | 1.10-1.20 | 0.88-0.94 |
| Wickstead four, 4 each | 0.97-0.99 | 1.23-1.26 | 1.15-1.25 | 0.88-0.95 |
| Glade duo, 3 each | 0.89-0.98 | 1.05-1.16 | 1.05-1.15 | 0.93-0.95 |
| Glade trio, 4 each | 0.93 | 1.18 | 1.10-1.20 | 0.95-0.98 |
| Glade four, 4 each | 0.91-0.93 | 1.16-1.18 | 1.15-1.25 | 0.89-0.94 |
| Sewer, as built (1 seed) | duo 1.14-1.21, trio 1.07, four 1.01-1.10 | 1.14-1.21, 1.18, 1.16-1.27 | | 0.73-0.94 |
Within about 0.02 of the band except the Thicket's tank + healer (1.00) and a Sewer duo or four's top. The trio is thin: one or two mixes a ground.

*What it costs.*
- **Pressing + pays less than the first table did.** Against a lone hero who also goes to 8 each, a four lands 1.04-1.14 (was 1.15-1.26), and a Wickstead duo 0.95-1.00, about level with solo. I chose the default because most players never press it. If Tyler wants pressing + to stay on target too, the table needs a second value for a picked pull.
- **Gold follows the pull with no bonus:** mixed net per head moves from 0.51-0.81 as built to about solo, 0.86-1.23, so parties create as much gold per head as lone heroes.
- **Server cost unmeasured:** a Thicket four's instance holds 2.7x the monsters as built. Run `settle-budget` before shipping.
- **Mill Cellar stays under solo:** 3 each only reaches 0.75-0.77 for a duo, and it is a tutorial room of about 20 minutes, so it is not worth a row. Its bonus numbers are unmeasured.
- **The Warrens:** as built a four is 0.86-0.88 of a lone hero on the same ground, but a lone hero there at Level 22 loses 6k to 30k g/h, and against the Glade the party already earns about 2x. No row.
- **Dice:** cells are 2-3 hero-hours, so about 3% either way. The guard should play these at more seeds.

*Safety at the new pulls* (2 h x 3 seeds a cell, 2 x 12 h for the long runs): **0 deaths in every cell.** Fresh heroes in the practice set: Thicket Level 5 typical 80-100%, Wickstead Level 8 typical 82-100%, worst tenth 59-91%. Glade floor in floor gear: 89-100%. Twelve-hour hunts: Wickstead Level 8 practice duo and four, Glade Level 16 duo, 24 hours each, 0 deaths, 0 dips under 25%. **One edge:** unarmored hammer parties at the Thicket's floor had 2 dips under 25% in 6 h (three hammers at 5 each, four at 6 each), about the ruling's limit of one per hero per 12 h. The practice set is not what the ruling calls properly equipped, and armor drops there within the hour. All other bars: above +25% over the bill.

*Exactly what changes* (for `lane/b-party-class-xp`):
1. The XP table in the lane's data row: the values above.
2. `src/data/hunt-grounds.js`: a new `partyPull` on three ground rows, `meadowbrook_thicket [0, 4, 5, 6]`, `wickstead [0, 3, 3, 4]`, `mosslight_glade [0, 3, 4, 4]`; absent = as built.
3. `densityFor(ground, partySize, pick)`, the same file: with no pick and more than one hero, answer `partyPull[n - 1]`, clamped as now. Size 1 is untouched, so the solo Level curve cannot move; `level-curve` must stay green. `combat-sim.js` calls it when the world opens, so a death or a leave never changes the pull mid-hunt.
4. `src/shell/hunt-list.js densityStep`: in a party, pick 0 now means the party's pull, so the steps must treat it as that size or a press would jump. `hunts.js` already shows the answer ("4 each"), and the note should say "Your party's pull".
5. It moves the edge bundle: both paths, one local run-smoke, one deploy.

*The hard line: a same-class party never beats solo per head.* Holds today on the Thicket, Wickstead, the Glade and the Warrens with no cap: best pull against the members' best pull alone, at most 0.99. **It breaks on the Sewer today**: 1.13-1.18 as built and up to 1.05 at the best pull, because the Rat King's hall opens only to a party. The cap is the Sewer's 0.80: its worst same-class cell (two bows, Level 8, as built) lands 0.95. x0.85 is not enough: it leaves that cell at 1.00. Dice: Sewer cells are one seed, so the engine lane's guard must play this at more seeds, on every ground, as built and at the full box, with the bar at 1.00.

*Edge cases.*
- **3 tanks + 1 healer:** two classes of four. 1.13 at the default pull lands 0.98 on Wickstead and the Glade; on the Sewer at 1.05 about 1.01 at 8 each (as built not measured). Three of four classes land 1.04-1.16. A partial mix earns part of the bonus and does not beat solo by much.
- **A death or a leave mid-hunt:** the next kill reads the living. Healer dead in a full four: 3 heroes, 3 classes, 1.27. No latch and no grace. A hero's class cannot change mid-hunt, since gear goes on in town. The default "Leave if anyone leaves" box sends most parties home anyway.
- **One player's own four heroes:** paid as four strangers are. Account total on Wickstead L13 at the default: about 140k Level XP/h, against 114k for the same four hunting alone as built (+22%) and 62k as a party today. Gold gets no bonus (about 1.0x per head). **Monitor: flag any account with 3+ own heroes in one party whose per-head Level XP over 20 hunted hours exceeds 1.30x that ground's solo pace.** Review if flagged accounts pass a quarter of party hours.
- **A low-Level hero brought along for its class:** counts while alive and takes its even share, so the others lose XP to it. Watch it in the same flag.
- **Not fixed by this:** a lone hammer at 8 each earns 71.9k on Wickstead against 54.4k for the mixed four. That is role parity (Tyler's open item), not this rule.

*Where a player sees it*, before Start with party, on the Hunts page's party block and the Party window's Your party tab, from the same table: "Your party has 2 of 4 classes: 15% more Level XP here. Melee, Ranger, Mage and Healer each count." On the Sewer, one class: "One class only: 20% less Level XP here." (Wickstead duo: 10%.) No-bonus cells: "Your party has 1 of 4 classes: no extra Level XP here." The pull line beside it already says bigger pulls pay. All of it ships with the engine change, so the words are never ahead of the game.

**(c) Hero power barely moves income.** Confirmed as built, and it is the walk again:
| Kills/h, tank | Fresh, practice set | Grown, in gear | As built | At 8 each |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Cellar | L1 | L20 | 470 to 494, +5% | 1,075 to 1,196, +11% |
| Thicket | L5 | L15 | 540 to 606, +12% | 855 to 1,032, +21% |
| Wickstead | L8 | L18 | 501 to 579, +16% | 717 to 943, **+32%** (XP 33.9k to 44.6k; net -347 to 6,253 g/h; the fresh hero dips 11 times and dies once in 4 h) |
Armor stays on target: 18-19.5% less damage a full tier (10-31 verdict). **A real weapon upgrade, measured** (the panel never tested one). Level 16 in Wickstead, armor and skills the same, 2 h x 3 seeds = 6 hero-hours a cell on the same dice, v0.2.78; before and after:
| Style, practice weapon to first real rung | Pull | Kills/h | Level XP/h | Gold + loot g/h | Store g/h | §8 (+5-8%) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sword to Iron Sword | as built | 544 to 572 (+5.1%) | 27,450 to 28,836 (+5.0%) | 3,721 to 3,684 | 0 to 0 | meets |
| | 8 each | 854 to 924 (+8.2%) | 40,439 to 43,754 (+8.2%) | 5,226 to 5,872 | 61 to 82 | meets |
| Hammer to Iron Warhammer | as built | 668 to 670 (+0.3%) | 33,688 to 33,831 (+0.4%) | 4,153 to 4,352 | 53 to 28 | misses |
| | 8 each | 1,551 to 1,612 (+3.9%) | 73,477 to 76,446 (+4.0%) | 9,755 to 9,970 | 257 to 242 | misses |
| Bow to Longbow | as built | 627 to 645 (+2.9%) | 31,659 to 32,543 (+2.8%) | 4,445 to 4,084 | 23 to 12 | misses |
| | 8 each | 1,032 to 1,098 (+6.4%) | 48,880 to 52,001 (+6.4%) | 6,354 to 7,259 | 200 to 190 | meets |
| Wand and book to Oak Wand and Oak Spellbook | as built | 673 to 676 (+0.4%) | 33,943 to 34,092 (+0.4%) | 4,590 to 4,350 | 252 to 190 | misses |
| | 8 each | 1,510 to 1,543 (+2.2%) | 71,575 to 73,076 (+2.1%) | 9,567 to 9,967 | 911 to 891 | misses |
Read kills and XP, which repeat closely. Gold + loot in 6 h is mostly dice on what dropped (it moves -8% to +14% with kills up 0-8%), so it shows no weapon effect either way at this sample. **The cause in the engine is overkill, then the walk.** A lone hero's monsters have 55% HP (`packScalePermille`, `hunt-grounds.js`): Wickstead's spawns have 7 to 18 HP, 10 of 12 at 16 or under. The median kill took no time after the first blow, on both weapons, for hammer and wand and nearly so for sword and bow, so a stronger blow lands on a monster that was dying anyway. The swing is a fixed 2.0 s (Tyler 10-15), so a weapon cannot speed that, and what is left of the hour is `pull-cycle.js`: walking to the next pack. Monsters that survive the first blow are where a weapon pays, which is why the Sword (the lowest hit) and full-box pulls show it. **Also:** nothing can be bought. The Shop sells no weapon, and from Level 1 to 14 there is no rung at all. The first ones are Level 15 drops: Iron Sword, Iron Warhammer, Longbow and Oak Wand are all listed from Sewer monsters, a party ground (Bramble Blade from Wickstead's Turncoats is the lone-hero sword). No number changed; a fix belongs with the pack-size work under (a), since more monster HP is the lever that makes a weapon, danger and a healer all count at once. Not run: tier 2 to 3, hatchets, staff, crossbow. **Recommend:** no number. Pull size is the optimizer's decision and it pays what a min-maxer wants; the line above now points at it. Raising the as-built pull instead would break the Level curve: Wickstead L13 earns 38% more XP at 8 each, and level-pace is fitted to as built. Risk of doing nothing: a player who never presses + still sees flat income. Watch `hunt_density` picks in vitals during the beta; if under a third of heroes past Level 10 touch it, make it louder in the Hunts window (art-director).

### 2026-10-31 Does the first arrow cost? (designer): BRONZE ARROWS STAY FREE. **This departs from a plain reading of Tyler's 10-15 words; he can overrule it**
**Ruling, exact numbers.** Bronze Arrows stay `ammoPerShot: 0`, rangeAtkB 2, rangeStrB 2, with no store row and `NO_STORE_PARTNER` as it is. The kit keeps 50. Paying starts at the next arrow, which already exists: Iron Arrows (Ranged 1, `ammoPerShot: 1`, rangeStrB 3, crit 1%, store 1 g a shot), then Barbed (Ranged 15, store 1 g a shot). No coin, floor or kit number moves. lane/a-arrow-cost's Bronze cost does not ship.

**Do Tyler's rulings reach the rung?**
- 10-23 ("when carried stock runs out... bought from the store"): no. It is conditional on running out, and Bronze never does. Every arrow that can run out already has its store row.
- 10-15 ("Arrows keep a per-shot cost"): by its plain words, yes, so this is the departure. "Keep" was said while Bronze was already free (since 08-14) and answered a question about staff and wand basics, so it can be read as "don't drop the cost arrows have". Nothing shows he had the free rung in mind. Newer beats older only if it reaches, so I am not claiming the point: it is his to overrule.

**Why, measured** (lane/a-arrow-cost, real settle, 1 h, practice bow, 3 seeds; other styles from the balance baseline above).
| | Cellar L1 | Thicket L5 | Thicket L15 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Income with free arrows, g/h | 1,765 | 1,949 | 2,358 |
| At 1 g a shot, g/h | 945 | 704 | 1,199 |
| Shots' share of income | 46% | 64% | 49% |

- 1 g is the smallest price the store has, so the 08-14 reasoning still holds: no integer price stays under a third of income.
- XP/h does not move, so the bill buys nothing. A Ranger would pay about 830 to 1,400 g/h on the first grounds, against melee 18 to 119 and the Mage 201 to 354, for the same pace (Ranger 1.00 to 1.08× against Mage 1.06 to 1.15×). In the Sewer party it pays 1,643 g/h.
- Coin alone doesn't cover it: my archer earned about 750 g/h in coin in the Cellar against an 820 g/h bill, so the income is unsold loot. With 500 g and the 300 g floor that is gold_out at minute 53 to 84, with 950 to 1,650 g of commons in the bag. A newcomer sent home broke by the style they picked is the same failure as the 0-kill archer.
- Keeping it free needs no migration, no Sewer coin change and no edge deploy before Sunday's beta; `first-grounds-net-chain` stays green.

**Rejected.** *Starter stack plus coin* (say 1,000 Bronze, Sewer coin +13%): moves the cliff to about hour 1, into the Thicket's 1,245 g/h bill, and moves a money surface days before the beta, unmeasured past the Sewer. *Cost starting at Barbed only*: that is this ruling minus Iron, which is already paid. *One arrow spent per five shots*: fair, but it needs engine work and doesn't fix the price of the Sewer.

**The other rungs.** The lane touched neither, and neither makes Bronze inconsistent. Air Runes no longer exist. Coarse Whetstones already wear at 0.02 a swing, with no store row, and a sword swings on without one. The rule is now: **a basic attack costs nothing you can run out of** (a swing is free, a caster's basic costs mana that comes back, Bronze is free); what is better costs. The comments at `src/data/slot-ladders.js:59` and `src/core/ammo.js:212` still say every tier-1 rung is free. They are stale on that point and change with the next edge deploy, since ammo.js is in the bundle.

**Walk-through: a fresh archer's first hour** (M = played on the fixed code, S = balance sim, P = projected from those rates).
| Minute | What happens | Holds | Why it works |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | "Every weapon is a style", press Practice Bow, Equip (M) | 500 g, 20 Cooked Shrimp, 50 Bronze | The same press loads Bronze; "You fight as a Ranger now" |
| 1 | Hunts, Mill Cellar: "Ammo: Bronze Arrows loaded. They never run out." Enter (M) | same | No arrow bill; juice 10 gp a drink |
| 1-2 | First kills: 5 in 21 s (M) | coin arriving | Bronze is loaded |
| 2-18 | Plant, bar and rune cards. Flame Volley stays struck unless the player picks a 1 g rune (M) | 7,355 Level XP/h, 1,958 g/h gold + loot, 159 g/h supplies (M) | Gold rises all the way: never near the 300 g floor |
| about 20 | Level 5, Cash in, Blessings free, Sell all loot about 250 g, cook, Tamsin (M, other styles; P for the archer) | about 900 g | Nothing here needs an arrow |
| 25-60 | Thicket: 24,572 Level XP/h, 1,949 g/h, 0 deaths in 228 h (S) | about 2,000 g in gold and unsold loot by minute 60, Level 6 to 7 (P) | No gold_out path: the bill is drinks |
| Any time | Wants more damage: press an empty slot, Ammo, Iron Arrows at 1 g a shot | | A real decision: free or stronger. Whether Iron is worth 1 g is **unverified** |

Not played: the Thicket as an archer on the fixed code. Next check for the balance sim: a Ranger on free Bronze against Iron and Barbed at each floor, so paid arrows are worth buying and free Bronze isn't best forever.

**If Tyler overrules:** keep lane/a-arrow-cost's cost, add 1,000 Bronze to the kit, Sewer coin +13%, and re-measure gold_out and net-chain before any beta.

**The no_fight stop ships regardless** (10 hunt minutes with no blow landed by anyone in the instance). Bronze free and Equip loading shut the door we know; a hero can still reach no attack another way (arrows moved to Storage, a setup with nothing loaded), and it would burn stamina and the store for hours. The lane measured normal quiet stretches well under 10 minutes, and the end card says what to check. It shares commit 037ad3d8a and the generated catalogue with the Bronze cost, so the lane must split them: drop `plain_bronze_arrows` and the `ammoPerShot` change, regenerate the catalogue, keep `no_fight`. That is a lane C server and edge change.

### 2026-10-31 Core loop, hours 1 to 20 and on (designer, lane/b-core-loop-20h): PLAYED on the local world, fixes in the lane
**Method.** The real `play.html` on `tools/qa/local-world.mjs` (the replayed chain, the real `hr-accrue`), v0.2.74 plus the a-andy-reports and live-view-robust fixes, 1440x900. Real sessions: an evening of 45-50 minutes, a hunt left running overnight with the browser closed, a morning and an evening return, with town time passing between (the harness's new `wait()`: stamina rests, crops grow, a gathering or bench job runs on; only waiting is compressed). **Marlow** (sword and shield, solo all the way): 27.6 h hunted over three days, Level 18. **Tessaly** (wand) and **Brin** (staff), one account, a party from Level 8: 11.7 h, Levels 14 and 13. Past hour 20: `tools/balance-sim/level-pace.json` and the 10-31 balance verdict (lane/b-balance-baseline), not re-measured. Shots: `art/review/core-loop-20h-2026-10-31/` (21).

**Verdict.** Yes for the first day, not yet for the second and third. The semi-idle promise holds: close the game, come back to a summary, Levels, a bag of loot and a purse; the Sewer party and the Glade at 16 (2.1x Wickstead's XP an hour, a Level an hour) are the two moments that feel like a game opening up. Between them the screen stops saying what you are working toward. On the hero's way from Level 8 to 15 (hours 2 to 18) it named one Thicket boss Rare, then nothing until the Iron tier; the coach sat on a drink card a sword hero never needed; Tamsin's armour was used up by one night of drops; gold climbed to 64k with nothing past a 360 gp ring to spend it on in sight; and when the Iron tier came, Equip upgrades would not put it on. Every system works; few tell the player why it matters at hour 10. Past hour 20 the solo road narrows to one ground, a flat 84k XP an hour and no gear from Level 30 to 50 (125 h), which is where the most active real player sits.

Equip upgrades + the Shop's jewellery: a good ten minutes. Tamsin paid the Bronze Helm, then offered 480 gp + 6 Bones |
| 12.5 h (eve 2) | 11 | 24,432 | Bronze set + Shop jewellery + Graypelt Cloak | "Drink your own juice" (one store juice drunk in 12 h) | Wickstead until stamina (30k XP/h, 3.9k gp/h); Dispatch sold 21 stacks for 4,377 gp in one press; the Homestead and its Garden (400 gp, 20 ore, 30 logs gathered in minutes) |

Ten Iron Platebodies, eleven Platelegs, an Iron Helm in the bag; Equip upgrades put on only the Torc (fixed below). Put Iron on by hand, then the shield and Bramble Blade (nothing points at them) |

Tessaly and Brin: the mage burns 519 gp/h of store runes and juice from minute one (54 runes and 12 juices in the Cellar's 20 minutes). A second hero at Level 6 of the first: Cellar 25 min, Thicket 3 h, both at 8 by about 4 h; right-click the tab, Invite to party, Start with party. Sewer duo, a mage and a healer with no tank: 47-50k XP/h each, 0 deaths in 7.4 h, 3,961 gp/h after a 600 gp/h bill (1,425 runes, 242 juices). At the same hours the party heroes are three Levels ahead of Marlow.

### 2026-10-31 First hour in every fighting style (designer, lane/b-first-hour-styles): PLAYED from the sign-in screen on the local world (tools/qa/local-world.mjs), shots art/review/first-hour-styles-2026-10-31/
Fresh accounts: sword + shield, hatchets, bow, wand + spellbook, staff, plus a two-hero party at 922x423 touch and a death on purpose. Waiting compressed with skip, results real. Then the blind panel's list (lane/f-*).

| Style | Cellar to Level 5 | Thicket | Falls | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sword + shield | 19 min, 147 kills | 75 min, Level 7 | 0 | PASS |
| Hatchets | 21 min, 185 kills | 20 min, 234 kills | 0 | PASS |
| Bow | **0 kills in 7 min with 50 arrows in the bag**; fixed: 5 kills in 21 s | not re-run after the fix | 0 | FAIL, then PASS on the fix |
| Wand + spellbook | 21 min, 182 kills | 20 min, 249 kills | 0 | PASS |
| Staff, solo (Tyler's P1) | 20 min, 137 kills, no food eaten | 20 min, 180 kills | 0 | PASS |
| Own party, phone | Sewer: L5 tank + L1 healer alive 10 min | | 0 | PASS once the tabs could be pressed |

**Rulings**
- **HP "+0.6/s":** the bar shows the rate a watcher can see: "+12/s rest". Between fights a hero recovers 20 times faster, and in a fight the blows drown the slow rate, so that one lives in the title. Mana unchanged.
- **"Order matters"** is now "Your own food first", and says what it costs. It and "Eat on time" wait until the hero has eaten three times in a hunt: at minute 9 a tank had eaten once, which was the skeptic's nearest refund.
- **A card that asks never walls.** Every first-hour card that asks for a press has a Got it. The Cellar's bar and farm lessons stand aside at Level 5, so Cash in always gets through, and return in the Thicket if undone.
- **"Next level in: after 10 min"** is hidden until the server has a rate; the Per hour line says when it comes.
- **A sword's first bar** (Challenge, Power Strike, food, food, drink) makes sense as it is. The trap was the store food after "Eat on time", now covered above.
- **Starter rune spells on other styles:** each is struck and each is explained three ways (Before you go, its slot, "Spells need runes"). Keep them off the store by default: the 10-29 measurement says store runes on every default slot cost a fifth of net gold and turned drop-tables red. The veteran's "her only damage spell is dead" overstates it: the staff's basic attack made all her kills for the first 4 minutes (31 by minute 5).
- **Order:** the weapon card comes first, before any hunt; Blessings come first on the way back. The Help page gets its own card. Leave's countdown shows no card.
- **Cards in a hunt** fold to their title after their reading time (20 s at least), unless the player opened them. Tamsin's payment and "ran out" lines leave after 30 s.
- **Zero-kill hunts should stop:** no kill in 10 minutes of fighting ends the hunt with its own reason and an end card that says why. It needs a new END_ORDER reason and an engine change: lane C, not built here. The door we know (bow) is shut, and Before you go warns in red.
- **Stamina:** both numbers are true. `STAMINA_MAX_MS` is 8 h free, 12 h premium, and beta_premium makes every hero 12 h. Help now says so.

**Creator colors: a missing render, not a bug.** The four picks reach the server (hr_create_hero). No world or hunt renderer reads them (no `colors` in src/world or src/hunt-view) and every outfit has `regions: []`, so no mask art exists. The real fix is mask frames per outfit (asset-director) plus one palette swap at sheet load: days of art, about a day of code. For now the step says so in one line. Decision for Tyler: keep that line until the art, or hide the color rows (CLAUDE.md §3.5 says half-built stays hidden; his 10-19 ruling asked for the step).

**Ranked, not built**
1. Zero-kill stop: built, the `no_fight` end (lane/a-no-fight, 2026-10-31: 10 hunt minutes with no blow landed by anyone in the instance).
2. Switching style with an own bar leaves the old style's moves struck beside the new ones. It says why, but a setup per style is the real answer: teach it at the first switch, not after the Thicket. Bites after hour 1.
3. Tamsin pays materials once the hero already owns her pieces, and by 200 wolves drops have usually supplied them. The card now says so; her greeting still promises armor. Giving the next tier is a lane C rule change.
4. A Level 1 sword in Wickstead (floor 8) reached Level 5 in 5 minutes with no death, against 20 in the Cellar: the warning is true but the push pays. Balance call for the sim, unverified.
5. Icon tooltips sit under the first card; town icons have no words. Art-director.
6. "Sell all loot" and "Dispatch loot" overlap in the first hour. Keep both for now; revisit with lane/b-core-loop-20h.
7. "Taking a beating?" fires at 3 meals, which is ordinary eating. Watch it now that "Eat on time" shares the trigger.
8. Invite to party's result stays in its menu until a tap elsewhere; on a phone that menu covers the hero tabs.

**The other weapons, first minutes on the fixed code:** hammer 57 kills, crossbow 30 (shield kept, arrows loaded), a wand with no book 5 in 25 s. A sword set to Chase off, Distance 3 still killed 17 in under 3 minutes, so that is no trap.

**Walk-through (each step from the kit and the steps before it; the machine check is tests/hunt-core/sewer.mjs `tutorial-walk-through` and `first-hour-never-walls`)**
| Step | Card | Holds and can reach | Doable because |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Every weapon is a style | kit: all practice weapons, worn sword and shield | Got it, or Equip in the bag |
| 2 | Rats in the mill | Hunts window; the Cellar is open | Enter (a bow loads the kit's arrows on Equip) |
| 3 | Plant your land | 8 Turnip Seeds, 2 plots, in a hunt | Farm plants during a hunt, or Got it |
| 4 | Your action bar, Spells need runes, A spell costs runes | default bar, store runes 1 gp, 500 gp | Configure action opens in a hunt; each has Got it |
| 5 | The whole picture | gear icon, Settings' Help tab | Got it |
| 6 | Out to the brook, Cash in | Level 5 at about 20 minutes (played) | Leave hunt |
| 7 | Before the wolves | free below Level 30 | Shop, Services, Bless |
| 8 | Sell, Decide once, Cook your catch | loot from step 6, 10 Raw Shrimp in the kit | Shop, bag, Gathering and crafting |
| 9 | Tamsin's wolves, Into the Thicket | Level 5, a weight to pick | Tally, then Enter |
| 10 | Eat on time, Your own food first | has eaten three times in this hunt | Configure action, or Got it |

**Card placement with lane/a-phone-reach and lane/a-numbers-agree in, fresh heroes (shots s922/, s667/):** at 922x423 and 667x375 no card ran off screen, or over the hero or Leave; cards folded in a hunt and Got it could be pressed. Found and fixed: the town card's cap was overridden, so it ran 30 px over the bar at 667; the first card's Got it fell off a phone's bag sheet; the creation step ran off the right edge at 667. The phone hero tabs are lane/a-phone-reach's fix; mine gave way to it.

**Not played:** the Thicket after the bow fix; real waiting on farm and cooking clocks; live. Not reproduced: one page's bag failed to open with three heroes running on the loaded PC; a fresh page with the same weapon was fine.

**Every L1-25 hard target holds on the path the game advises.** On the grounds each group is sent to, in band and as built: 0 deaths and 0 close calls in gear for every way to fight, and 0 deaths in the practice set. 376 of 376 twelve-hour hunts finished, 136 of them in the practice set; the only 3 close calls were the practice-set party tank at the Sewer floor. Every hunt clears +25% over the bill. The thinnest is the Mage: +757% in gear, +371% in the practice set. The solo Healer had 0 falls in the Cellar and the Thicket (48 h each, practice staff). Gear lands 0.75-1.9 pieces an hour per slot per weight at every floor; the Cellar drops only the tutorial's helmet. Each Rare item comes about every 10.5-13.5 h: one Rare of any kind every 0.9-2.1 h, spread over 5-15 Rare rows if they share evenly (drop-tables `rare-pace` pins each row's own pace); the Cellar's single Rare every 11.6 h. Max density is 8 everywhere, and every new hero owns all six starter sets. The solo path is Cellar → Thicket at 5 → Wickstead at 8 → Glade at 16; a pair goes Cellar → Sewer at 8. A fresh duo or four in practice sets at the Sewer floor (the tutorial's "Into the sewer, together") had 0 deaths in 48 h; its tank sat in Normal (typical 61% in the first hour, 77% over 12 h). The one L1-25 practice-set cell under +25% is the Glade floor: the tank +16%, the sword alone −17%. That hero would be about 25 h in, already holding Thicket and Wickstead armour.

**Top 10 discrepancies, by what a first-week player feels (first met first)**
| # | Met at | What the player meets | Measured | Lever |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | L5 | No danger anywhere, so a ground above your Level pays more for nothing | In band every hunt is Easy (typical minimum 93-100%; Normal is 60-80), and Hard and Extreme do not exist in gear. Under a floor is no different. A Level 6 hero in the Glade (10 under; the server refuses only from 15 under) earns 60.5k Level XP/h with 0 deaths in 48 h in gear and 48 h in the practice set, against about 25k in the Thicket where the game sends it. L11 in the Glade: 62.5k vs about 31k at Wickstead. L22 in the Warrens: 113k vs about 75k in the Glade. "Expect losses" is not true, and a min-maxer skips the fitted pace | engine (the danger layer) |
| 2 | L8 | A fresh party leader slides the Sewer to the full box | Practice-set duo: 13 deaths in 4.5 h, four: 23 (the tank), and the tank's bill eats its coin (+19% duo, −1% four). In gear: 0 deaths | advice (the slider) |
| 3 | L8 | A party earns less per hero than going alone | At its better density, against the comparable solo where a lone hero is sent: duo 0.72-0.95×, four 0.69-0.90× on the Thicket, Wickstead and the Glade (less at the Cellar, and at Wickstead's top against the Glade). At the Sewer floor the duo is 1.27× (target 1.05-1.15), the four 1.06× (target 1.15-1.25). A duo out-earns a four everywhere | system (XP share) |
| 4 | L8 | A lone fresh hero enters the Sewer | Practice set alone at L8: the tank 3 deaths and 28 close calls in 6 h, axes 31 and hammer 21 deaths in 6 h each (Culvert Bats, Cistern Spiders). The card already says "A party of 2 to 4" and the game never sends a lone hero there; in Thicket gear, 0 deaths | off advice |
| 5 | L15 | The first smithed weapon is barely felt | Tier 1 → 2 at L16 (played at Wickstead), Level XP/h as built: sword +5%, healer +3.5%, bow and crossbow +2.7%, axes +0.8%, hammer and Mage +0.4% (target +5-8%). At the full box single-target styles gain +6-8.4% (meets the target); hammer, axes and Mage +2-4%. As built, XP/h is the ground's spawn cap, so a better weapon cannot show. Tier 2 → 3 at L31 has the same shape | follows #1 |
| 6 | L16 | The Sewer party is outclassed by the solo Glade | At 17-25 a Sewer party earns 0.65-0.74× per hero what a lone hero earns in the Glade; a party in the Glade earns 0.86-0.93× | system (#3) |
| 7 | L5-31 | The shield tank and the Healer level slowest | Level XP × the pace: tank 0.87-0.96 at the first floors and 0.77 in the Glade; Healer 0.82-0.91; Mage 1.06-1.15 | role parity (open, Tyler's) |
| 8 | L5-31 | Even the full box is mostly Easy | Typical minimum at the full box: Thicket 89-98, Wickstead 76-97, Glade 70-91, Sewer party 80-100; only melee and tank reach Normal | follows #1 |
| 9 | L32 | No solo path past the Glade | A lone hero is kept in the Glade from 32 to 50 (the Warrens is a party ground with soloFrom 45, which recommendedHunt ignores); a Warrens party earns 2.0-2.3× that | above 25 (D5, known) |
| 10 | L45 | No tier-4 gear to reach | No weapon or armour step from L30 to 50+ | above 25 (known) |

Armour meets its target per piece: one body piece one tier up cuts damage per monster swing by 5.1-5.8%; a full tier cuts it by 18-19.5%.

**Fixes: none.** No L1-25 hard target fails on the advised path. Every role clears the first grounds, no ground is under +25%, no starter tank dies on a ground it is sent to, and there is no level wall (level-curve C6 green). Every miss above needs one of four things, and none is a data-row defect inside this lane's brief: an engine term (#1, #5, #8), a system rule Tyler holds (#3, #6, #7), advice copy (#2, #4), or work above Level 25 (#9, #10). **Ranked proposals (not built):**
1. **The danger layer (#1, #5, #8).** A level-gap term on monster atk under a ground's floor. The 10-26 run saw deaths appear at +8% a Level, steeply and differently by style; it needs a calibration of 100 h a cell. Price the full box with it, so that Normal, Hard and Extreme exist. Stop-gap if wanted before the beta: `SESSION_LIMITS.belowRangeRefuse` 15 → 5 (Tyler's 10-18 floor rule; lane C, `hr_hunt_limits`).
2. **Party XP share (#3, #6).** The 10-26 proposal m(n) = 1 + 0.055 × (n − 1), through xp-share's per-hero multiplier, puts a four at the full box at 1.20× on the party grounds. It re-opens Tyler's 10-22 "no party bonus", so it is Tyler's call. XP credit is a ranked surface: it goes into the EA Security review.
3. **Name the danger where the choice is made (#2, #4).** The density slider names the difficulty for the worn set; the Sewer card names it for a lone hero in practice gear. Advice only: designer and art lanes.
4. **Re-measure the weapon step (#5) after 1.** Role parity (#7) stays Tyler's open item.

**Standing guard (new): `tests/hunt-core/first-week-bands.mjs`** (in run-all; about 40 s on one core). Cases: each of the seven ways to fight, alone in the practice set, at the floor of every ground the Hunts window sends a lone hero to on day one (Cellar 1, Thicket 5, Wickstead 8); and a practice-set duo and four at the first party floor (Sewer 8). The bars are absolute, never pinned: no death; no dip under 25% alone, at most one in a party; typical encounter ≥ 60% alone and ≥ 50% for the party tank; +25% over the bill; Level XP at least half the pace. 4 mutants. Culvert Bat atk 9 → 12 is caught only here: drop-tables, sewer, conditions and forest-cave stay green, because their heroes wear the ladder kit.

### 2026-10-28 Greenhaven's sixth ground: Wickstead, 8-18 solo (designer, lane/b-greenhaven-6): RULED, STAGED until its painted ground
- **Band 8-18, solo first.** The 10-28 new-player walk found one solo ground for 6-15 (the Thicket) and no gloves, belts or jewellery past the Shop's basics. A 50+ capstone serves nobody before the playtest; a second 8-18 door is met in a new hero's first day. Floor 8 is where the first-hour rung lands; top 18 meets the Glade's 16.
- **Place:** Wickstead (Old English *wic*, a farm), a burned farmstead on the Thicket road where the Meadowbrook leaves the wood. Rick Yard, Granary, the footbridge (the one choke), Barn, Orchard, Burned House, the ford back, Cart Shed.
- **Creatures** (density-4 head-count): Granary Rat 14 common (swarm), Barn Wasp 4 uncommon (flier, weak to bows), Barn Spider 4 uncommon (tank, weak to fire), Turncoat 2 rare (deserters from a free company; the jewellery carrier). No boss. Every creature reuses a shipped style-1 unit (rat, hive_wasp, cistern_spider, glade_poacher). The ground needs one painted map in the Meadowbrook tileset family, as the Glade's was. That is the asset lane's job; it is STAGED until then (§3.5).
- **Loot:** tier-1 boots (rats) and helmets (wasps), tier-2 legs (spiders) and bodies plus the Bramble Blade (Turncoats), each piece every 1.3-1.5 h. The Rares, each every ~15 h, are the first tier-2 gloves (spiders), belts (wasps) and the Banded Signet, Wolfbone Torc and Fang Studs (Turncoats). The Shop owns those slots' basics, so here they are only ever the prize above them (forest-cave shop-outfits-defense). 10 rows leave `UNREACHABLE.greenhaven` for `staged` (ceiling 125 -> 115). They stay unsourced: tier-1 gloves and sashes (rare-worthy only as Shop twins), tier 3, the Tusker's Charm and Packlord's Band (a boar and a gnoll, which have no style-1 art), and bear meat and pelts (no bear art).
- **Measured at the floor** (real settle, solo Melee, ladder kit, six seeds): 0 deaths; net 2,340 g/h (the Thicket at 5: 1,850); 24.0k Level XP/h (the Thicket: 20.9k; the Sewer's party at 8: 34.4k); the bill is about 10 g/h, so the margin is far above +25%. The start kit alone lives the hour on all six seeds with a 199 g bill and nets 2,146 g. Total value per hero-hour is 3,150 against a 2,707 pin (interpolated from the Thicket to the Glade), +16%. Gear is 45% of coin. A fresh hero holds all four slots in every weight within 6 h. Was open: it nets more per hero than the Sewer's party (2,024). The party pays in XP; re-judge when the Sewer's coin is re-tuned.
- **RULED 2026-10-28 (designer): KEEP Wickstead's gold.** At Level 8 a solo hero earns more gold there; the Sewer's party earns 44% more Level XP (34.4k vs 24.0k), the Rat King, and the tier-2 helmets and boots. Gold for XP is a real choice, and both clear +25%. Tuning Wickstead down would only make the first solo hour worse. The lever is the Sewer: if it ends up empty, raise its coin; never cut Wickstead. **Re-judge** if two days of vitals show heroes leaving the Sewer for Wickstead. Next batch (lane/b-unsourced-items): the tier-1 gloves and sashes in the other weights are now made (Smithing 4 gauntlets, Crafting 2-4 gloves, sash and leather belt), and the tier-3 gloves and belts are Warrens Rares. The Orchard Boar (Wickstead's Orchard, uncommon, the orchard's tank) and the Brook Bear (Mosslight's Springs and Vine Hollow, uncommon bruiser; Raw Bear Meat and Bear Claw feed cook_bear_meat and cook_bear_pie) are wired on their art (lane/b-boar-bear-art). Still unsourced: the Tusker's Charm, which stays Old Tusker's champion relic (one source); its door is Old Tusker joining the Orchard with his own art. The Bear Pelt waits in `later` with its only dish, the Hunter's Feast (troll hide).

### Balance design ruling (Tyler, 2026-10-25): APPROVED TARGETS, pending real-engine validation
Tyler's document "HEARTHRISE — BALANCE DESIGN RULING" (2026-10-25) is the balance reference. Summary of what it rules (the full text is kept verbatim in the Coordinator's memory `balance-ruling-2026-10-25`; this summary is binding where it is specific):
- **Method.** Balance against REAL-ENGINE measurements only (combat-sim.js + src/data), never spreadsheets. Combat first, economy after. Audit the current code before changing anything; report the baseline; propose changes with measured justification; Tyler approves significant gameplay/economy changes; re-run regression sims after each change. Do not redesign working systems because older docs differ. Goal is not equal numbers; it is rewarding long progression, meaningful tradeoffs, reliable unattended hunting, preparation for hard content, a sustainable economy.
- **Levels (unlimited), cumulative hunting hours at a 12 h/day baseline WITHOUT purchased stamina:** L10 6 h · L25 36 h · L50 168 h (~2 wk) · L100 600 h (~50 d) · L200 1,680 h · L300 3,120 h · L500 6,720 h (~1.5 y) · L750 13,440 h · L1000 23,520 h (~5.4 y). Measure cumulative and per-level hours. Stamina: free 8 h, premium 12 h, 1:1 regen (purchased stamina evaluated separately). Frequent secondary rewards so no long stretch feels empty.
- **Difficulty bands (deaths per 1,000 h; typical min HP):** Easy 80–95%, ≤0.1 · Normal 60–80%, ≤1 · Hard 35–60%, 10–30 · Extreme 15–40%, 50–100. Normal hunt acceptance: ≤1 death / 1,000 h; ≤1 dip below 25% per 12 h (one continuous encounter = one close call; assess by HP% and time-to-death); a 12 h unattended hunt almost always finishes; sustainable healing; no infinite-sustain loops; danger rises predictably. Enough samples + confidence intervals for rare events. NO automatic XP penalty for lower-level monsters. Healing (incl. food on a 2 s cooldown): measure before proposing any nerf.
- **Death penalty:** preserve (2% combat XP; blessings reduce it and are a gold sink). Measure what an unblessed death costs in hunting hours per level.
- **Party XP:** a well-optimized 4-hero party earns 15–25% more XP per character than an efficient solo (illustrative: duo 105–115, trio 110–120, four 115–125; duo/trio provisional). Emerges from play, not necessarily a flat multiplier. Solo stays a legitimate path; no composition dominates everywhere; watch one player running four heroes.
- **Party settlement:** Mode A individual (default): own costs, own drops. Mode B shared cost/profit only with UNANIMOUS consent: pooled eligible costs and proceeds, net split equally, fair to whoever paid; rares stay with their winner. Join/leave/change-preference policy must be explicit, not invented. Report party profit per character and per controlling player; separate gold created/destroyed from gold transferred.
- **Rare loot:** keep the existing 0–100 roll with rotating eligibility (winner leaves the pool until all have won, then reset); the winner owns it even under Mode B. Audit membership change, death and session-boundary behaviour before changing it.
- **Weapons:** level requirements are current. A meaningful upgrade every ~15–25 levels = +5–8% MEASURED hunting performance (XP/h, damage, kill speed, supplies, profit), not +5–8% attack; sidegrades allowed; check against level/skills/spells/imbuements/monster curves (no blind compounding). Track per upgrade: required level, realistic level obtained, measured benefit, hours/gold to acquire.
- **Armour:** a meaningful upgrade = 3–5% less actual incoming damage under equal conditions; also measure healing, survival, supplies, profit, access to harder content; not judged by XP/h; no near-invulnerability.
- **Re-audit before use:** weapon configs, 1h vs 2h, shields/Defense, level requirements, rarity/classes, imbuements, roles, item and monster stats. Do NOT implement earlier shield-reduction or two-handed-bonus ideas without renewed approval.
- **Simulator outputs per scenario:** XP/h, gold/h, supply cost/h, profit/h, damage dealt/received/mitigated, healing throughput + resources, deaths, close calls, time to kill, hours to milestones; parties add total + per-character XP and profit and rare-allocation fairness. Start by measuring today; CI balance guards added gradually with statistical tolerances.
- **Economy (next phase):** gold created by hunting vs removed by supplies, blessings, services, crafting fees, other sinks; gear costs; stamina purchases; item/material flows; daily/monthly wealth; player-day models from real hunting output; faucets/sinks separate from player trades; market analysed separately; the 10-18 ChatGPT sim is reference only.
- **NOT approved (open):** role parity; combat-skill scaling vs level and gear; weapon-specific mechanics and shield tradeoffs; party gold profit vs solo; acquisition/rarity targets; shared-settlement treatment of common materials/unsold non-rares; party membership edge cases; simulator tolerances and sampling. Earlier assistant suggestions on these are not decisions.
**Coordinator's first audit (2026-10-25, from the code):** stamina 8 h / 12 h with 1:1 regen MATCHES (`STAMINA_MAX_MS`, src/data/hunt-sessions.js); death loss 2% with blessings MATCHES; rare roll with rotation EXISTS (`rareRoll` + `world.rareWon`, src/core/hunt-rewards.js; party-split.js); no low-level XP penalty found, MATCHES. **CONFLICT: the level curve.** LEVEL_CURVE (quartic 1, cubic 20, linear 50; src/data/core-model.js) with the design XP rate 2,900 × Level per hour gives L10 1 h (target 6), L25 12 h (36), L50 78 h (168), L100 543 h (600), L200 4,017 h (1,680), L500 59,612 h (6,720), L1000 468,363 h (23,520): too fast to L50, about right at L100, far too slow past L200 (hours per level grow ~L² today; the targets grow ~L). Design-model numbers, not engine-measured: the simulator's first job is to measure real XP/h per band and confirm.
### Real town plan (2026-10-25)
(game-designer, lane/d-real-town; Tyler 10-25: heroes stack in town, build the real town, no stopgap; built ON Town Blueprint v0.5, `docs/design/refs/TOWN-BLUEPRINT-v0.5-2026-10-15.png`. Mocks: `art/review/real-town-2026-10-25/`.)

**Why they stack today (root cause):** `src/data/world/maps/town_common.js` is a 24×14 corner with ONE `start` tile [11,9]. Every hero back from a hunt, and every unviewed (parked) hero, stands at that arrival tile (`TownPresence.stand(this._arrival)`, `src/session/town-presence.js`), and the server stores whatever tile the client publishes (`hr_town_move`, 2026-10-25-town-walk.sql). No activity has a place, so 4 heroes and every peer share one tile.

**Grid (REVISED for 60 players, Tyler 10-25 "expand the spacing"; replaces the 96×58 first cut).** The blueprint is kept exactly as Tyler drew it and scaled **×2**: one blueprint square (16 px) = 2 × 2 world tiles, so every site keeps its place and shape, every blueprint edge lands on a whole tile, and every road, yard and plaza doubles. (×1.5 was tried first: the market plaza came out 15 tiles deep between general_store and depot, too small for 24 × 24 without moving Tyler's depot.) Blueprint 84 × 58 → **168 × 116 tiles**, plus the **Westwood**, 24 tiles west of the shack wall = **192 × 116 tiles** (x −24..167, y 0..115; coordinates below are on this grid). One desktop screen at ×2 = 22 × 14 tiles; a phone 844×390 at ×2 = 13 × 6.

**Capacity (sizing rule).** Town instance = **60 players**, each fielding up to 4 heroes: **240 bodies worst case**, ~120 realistic. Stands are sized at **≥ 1.25 × each station's ruled worst case**; the town holds **≈ 545 stands** against 240 bodies, so a free stand always exists. **Roads:** the gatehouse road 6 tiles wide, the east-west road and the bridge 8 wide; every station's stands sit OFF the road; Hearth Square keeps two 2-tile aisles open (bridge → stalls along y51; shop → storage along x110-111). At 3.2 tiles/s the gatehouse → hearth walk is ~54 tiles = 17 s and the Westwood ~40 s: display only, no output waits on a walk.

**Layout: every place, why it is there, what a tap opens** ("worst" = bodies ruled to be there at once; stands ≥ 1.25 × worst)

| **Gatehouse** (x74-93 y10-21) | arrival yard x70-96 y22-27; road x80-85 south to the east-west road | hunt departure + arrival (every overground ground) | 40 → 50 | Hunts | a hunt ending for many heroes lands here at once |
| **The mill** on P08 (x86-99 y22-33, astride the river) + cellar hatch (88,34) | `mill` + `hatch` | Mill Cellar departure | walk onto the hatch | Hunts on Mill Cellar | the river turns the wheel; first hunt by the arrival road |
| **sewer_works** (x44-59 y56-67), grate (50,68) | grate | The Sewer departure | walk onto the grate | Hunts on The Sewer | blueprint site |
| **Smithy yard** (smithy x66-83 y36-47; west yard x54-65 y36-47; front y48 on the road edge) | 4 forges + 8 anvils (west yard), 8 work benches + 2 grindstones (front) | **Smithing**, **Crafting** | 30 → 40 (20 + 20) | skill `smithing` / `crafting` | across the bridge on the main road |
| **Tavern kitchen** on P07 (x62-73 y20-29) | cook yard x60-74 y30-34, 8 cook fires (2×1) | **Cooking** | 20 → 26 | skill `cooking` | by the gatehouse: hunters eat on the way in and out |
| **Training yard** (x60-79 y62-77) | 8 dummies | town combat training (10-14) | 18 → 24 | Hunts > Training | blueprint site |
| **Shore + fisher_guild** (guild x66-83 y78-89) | shore y90-93 x50-100 (sea fish); river bank x86-87 y56-71 (trout); harbor piers later | **Fishing** | 30 + 10 → 40 shore + 13 bank | skill `fishing` | sea fish from the sea, river fish from the river |
| **The Mine** (Mountain, east) | mouth x136-143 y34-42, off the square's east road (y40-43); quarry floor x144-162 y30-46, 24 rock nodes (copper … coal, essence) | **Mining** | 24 → 30 | skill `mining` | the only rock in town |
| **Rune block** at the mine | x150-157 y48-49, 4 blocks | **Runecrafting** | 12 → 15 | skill `runecrafting` | essence is mined there |
| **The Westwood** (west of the shack wall) | x −24..−1, y 16-90; 40 trees, tiers deepen westward; entered by the west road (y48-55) through an 8-tile gap in the wall | **Woodcutting** | 30 → 40 (one per tree) | skill `woodcutting` | west of the shacks |
| **Farm plots** (grass between the shacks and the shore) | x20-55 y82-89, 12 plots of 3×2 | **Farming** | 24 → 30 | window `farm` | open grass by the water |
| Homes (P01-P15) | blueprint lots ×2 | housing; 3 EA homes on P13-P15 | — | window `house` / home map | blueprint |
| **Total** | | | **≈ 545 stands for 240 bodies** | | |

There is no fletching, brewing, leatherwork, alchemy or construction skill in `src/data/skills.js`: none gets a place.

**Activity → station → stand (never overlap).** Each station row holds an ordered list of stands (primary, then overflow). The **server assigns the stand**: when a character's town activity changes (set_activity gather/artisan/training, a window station tap, a hunt start or end), the server picks the lowest free stand at that station in that shard and holds `(map, shard, station, stand, since)` with a unique key, so two heroes (yours or anyone's) can never hold one stand. A full station's next hero gets its overflow stands, then the nearest free square bench. ≈ 545 stands > 240 bodies, so a free stand always exists. The activity → station table (gather node → Westwood / Mine / Shore; artisan skill → Smithy / Tavern / Rune block / Hearth; training → yard; hunt → its departure point) is data in `src/data/world/stations.js`, mirrored into a server catalogue by the gen-catalogues pattern (a twin guard pins them).

**Bodies on the server and on screen (60 players, 240 bodies).** TODAY: `hr_town_walkers` returns at most **c_cap = 40** walkers per read, scanning c_scan = 80 rows (`supabase/migrations/2026-12-10-town-walker-role.sql`, unchanged since 2026-10-25-town-walk.sql), and the client mirrors it (`TOWN_PRESENCE.cap: 40`, `src/data/town-presence.js`). There are NO shards yet: one zone per map, and a fuller zone shows only its 40 most recent movers. MUST BECOME: (1) a town **instance of 60 players / 240 bodies**; a 61st player opens a second instance of the same map (a zone column on the walker row); friends and parties are placed together. (2) An **area-of-interest read**: the walkers within the viewer's view plus an 8-tile margin (a 38 × 30 window on desktop), capped at 120 per read, instead of the whole map. Standing bodies change only when their activity changes, so the read also takes `since` and returns only changed rows plus ids that left (a delta). (3) Rule if the measurements below fail: a player's UNVIEWED heroes stand at their stations up to **2 per player per station**; the rest draw no body and show as a count on that player's plate ("Brannoc +2"). Viewed heroes, party members and friends always draw. **Measure before L2 ships (three pillars: optimization):** frame time for units + plates at 40 / 120 / 240 bodies on screen, 1440×900 and 844×390, in the browser pane (`tools/qa/pane-probe.js`). Budget: ≤ 4 ms/frame for units on a mid phone, plates cached as bitmaps. Read cost at 60 viewers polling every 2 s (30 reads/s, ≤ 120 rows each, payload KB). Postgres time per AOI read at 240 rows.

**Server vs display (§1/§6).** SERVER: which station and stand each body holds, the free tile a steered hero publishes (as today, `hr_town_move`), and departures (the body walks to its exit and leaves the walker list). An UNVIEWED hero gets its stand from the server's activity pointer, so a parked or offline hero stands at its fire, not at the gate. DISPLAY ONLY: the walk between tiles (client pathing at 3.2 tiles/s, `steer-local.js`), idle poses, burn and throw effects, nameplate stacking. **No output depends on the walk**: the server starts the activity on the intent (identical output, manual or not); the walk (≤ 5 s inside a district) plays from the server's accepted answer, and the work animation starts on arrival. Starting a hunt switches the viewed hero's screen to the hunt at once; peers see the body walk to the gatehouse and leave.

**Walking rules.** A hero moves only because its player chose something (a station tap, a window that names a place, an activity, Fuel the hearth, a steer); Tyler's "never walks on its own" (10-19) holds. Back from a hunt it arrives at the gatehouse stand the server gives it, then walks to the station of the town activity the player chose to resume (10-20 ruling), else stays. Paths keep to roads and the apron; nobody cuts through a building or another hero's stand. Nameplates of neighbours step up a line instead of overlapping (mock 2).

**Phone (844×390, ×2, 13 × 6 tiles).** Same map, same zoom, camera on the viewed hero: every station's working face + its stands fit in 13 × 6 tiles, so the phone always shows the hero's whole station (the hearth's chimney runs off the top; its firebox, alcoves and both fuel rows fit: mock 3). No desktop compromise: the phone gets no special layout. Windows stay slide-out sheets; the world never re-centres for them (10-19).

**Art (style 1 only, through `tools/world-art`; art-director lane building now, generation approved 10-25).** District by district: (1) Hearth Square: the Great Hearth (footprint 11 × 4, art 11 × 11 tiles = 352 × 352 art px: chimney, firebox, two log alcoves, `building`/`station` class row in art-budget), flagstone apron tiles, benches, square ground, general_store / depot / barracks facades, 2 counters reuse `counter`; (2) Gatehouse + main road + bridge + river; (3) Smithy yard (reuse `smithy`, `forge`, `anvil`, `workbench`, `grindstone`), tavern + cook fire; (4) Mine face + rock nodes + rune block; (5) Westwood trees (reuse `pine`/`oak`, add tier trees); (6) Shore, fisher_guild, piers, farm plots; (7) shacks, homes. Tamsin's own sprite replaces the crier. Burn effect spec in the Hearth section.

**Mocks** (`art/review/real-town-2026-10-25/`, drawn by `mock.html` from live style-1 sprites; flat labelled blocks = art not made yet): `1-blueprint-overview-1440.png` (rev 2: the blueprint ×2 with every place, stand and the arrival route), `2-hearth-square-1440.png` (rev 2: 41 heroes in a 24 × 24 square), `3-hearth-square-phone-844x390.png` (rev 2), `4-storyboard-fuel-1440.png` (the interaction; drawn on the first-cut geometry, still valid), `5-named-fuel-1440.png` (rev 2). Crowd rule shown in mock 2: your heroes' plates always draw; another player's plate draws only where it fits (art-director handoff: plate density at 40+ bodies).

**Lanes (sizes):** L1 **map + stations data** (B, M): town_common becomes the 192×116 map (blueprint ×2 + the Westwood) in districts, stations rows with stands, TAP_OPENS rows; placeholder ground until art lands, district by district. L2 **server stands** (C, M, systems + backend): stand allocation table + `hr_town_walkers` derives station/stand from the activity pointer; §4 self-check (no two bodies on one stand; parked hero at its station). L3 **walk + work poses** (B, M): the client walks to the assigned stand from the server answer, plays the work pose, plate stacking; both-paths tests (viewed / unviewed). L4 **Fuel the hearth** (C+B, M): see Hearth section. L5 **art districts** (art-director, L, in flight). L1 and L5 start now; L2 before L3; L4 after L2.

### The Hearth: Inner Flame replaces Prayer (2026-10-25)
(game-designer, final design authority; Tyler 10-25: Prayer + burying bones lifts too much from OSRS; name "Inner Flame" is Tyler's pick; Inner Flame KEEPS death XP loss like Prayer, ruled.)

**The loop:** feed the Hearth, raise your Inner Flame. The town hearth kindles the hero's own flame; the warmth carries into the hunt. **What a level gives: unchanged job**: HP and mana regeneration RATE on the diminishing curve (`PRAYER_REGEN`, never doubles, so food and drink stay needed). It keeps Prayer's consumers (companion owl at 50, perks `prayerSpeed` → hearth speed, blessings unaffected). It stays a COMBAT skill for death loss (Tyler 10-25). **Copy rule:** Inner Flame is warmth and recovery, never fire damage or burn: its tooltip says "Your hero recovers health and mana faster" and never "fire"; it must not sit beside Flame Strike / Flame Volley (`src/data/abilities.js`) in any list, and its icon is a hearth, not a flame bolt.

**Fuel ladder (replaces the bury rites; one verb, "Fuel the hearth").** Drop tables keep every bone row: creatures with skeletons drop bones; a creature with NO skeleton drops its family's fuel instead (Tyler 10-25), decided by body, not by today's table.

| Tier | Fuel | XP (pre-dial) | Where it drops |
|---|---|---|---|
| Everyday | Bones / Big Bones / Dragon Bones / Bone Chips (today's rows, levels 1/15/35/40) | 40 / 15→ retuned / 72 / 105 (the Prayer pass retunes the ladder) | every skeleton (38+ rows, unchanged; "bonfire" is "bone-fire": folklore, not a lift) |
| Family (new, bones-tier XP 40, level 1) | **Lamp Jelly** (slimes: slime, sump_slime; slime's wrong bones row becomes this) | 40 | Mill Cellar, The Sewer |
| | **Dry Husk** (insects and spiders: hive_wasp, cistern_spider) | 40 | Mill Cellar, The Sewer |
| | **Pithwood** (walking plants: walking_vine; later mandrake, bog_vine, dryad, carnivorous_plant) | 40 | Mosslight Glade |
| | **Clinker** (golems, elementals) / **Wisp Wax** (spirits: wraith, void_mote) | 40 | designed now, ship with their first reachable ground |
| Higher rungs | today's offering rows (grave dust … level 99) keep their inputs and levels; their names drop the rite verbs and the banned filler ("Ancient", "Void") in the Prayer pass | as today | as today |
| Skill fuels (catch-up only) | logs (any tier) and coal | normal log 8, coal 14, +4 a tier | Woodcutting / Mining. **They only warm a flame below 0.6 × the hero's Level** (the design curve, `PRAYER_BURY`); above it they refuse with the reason. Measured why: a woodcutter makes ~750 logs/h vs ~121 bones/h on an in-range hunt (1.9k XP/h), so any useful log XP would let a gatherer out-train a hunter on an uncapped combat skill. Catch-up keeps Woodcutting and Mining a real consumer for the hero who sold its bones, and never breaks the curve. |
| **Named fuels (Tyler: "awesome")** | below: a burst of XP and the square's flame changes for everyone | ~1 h of that ground's bones | one per ground or boss, super-rare |

**Named fuels: the sample of 5** (one per live ground; each is a new item with a source and a use; sample before more):

| Fuel | Drops from | Rate | XP (pre-dial) | The flame (everyone in the square) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Queen's Comb** (wax comb from the hive's heart) | hive_wasp, Mill Cellar | 1 in 2,000 | 1,500 | honey-gold, fat sparks, a low hum; 60 s |
| **Rat King's Knot** (the tangle of tails) | rat_king, The Sewer | 1 in 150 kills | 3,000 | sewer-gas blue, a sharp pop, then a hiss; 60 s |
| **Den Knot** (resin-soaked pine from the den) | dire_gray, Meadowbrook Thicket | 1 in 400 | 3,000 | deep amber, resin spits and crackles; 60 s |
| **Verdigris Cake** (the copper scale off his stolen pots) | chief_coppercap, Coppercap Warrens | 1 in 150 kills | 4,500 | copper GREEN (copper salts really burn green), a ringing tone like a struck pot; 90 s |
| **Mosslight Clump** | walking_vine, Mosslight Glade | 1 in 1,500 | 4,500 | cold blue-green glow with drifting motes, near silent; 90 s |

The drop is a super-rare moment (reveal, broadcast, card; memory `super-rare-drop-moments`); the burn is a second moment: a named fuel always burns alone (never in "Fuel all"), the square's flame takes its colour for everyone present for 60-90 s, and the square's chat line says "Harrow fed the Hearth a Verdigris Cake." A second named fuel during a colour queues behind it. Rates are proposals at design grade (no sim yet): the balance-sim lane measures them.

**Fuel the hearth (the interaction, Tyler 10-25).** The item menu gets **Fuel the hearth** (one) and, on a stack, **Fuel all N**. Either sends ONE intent: the existing artisan activity (today's `bury_bones` path: a server-run bench at 640 ms an item, the bag and XP move each settle, the ledger journals it). The server answers yes or no; only a yes starts the hero's walk to its fuel stand (from the answer, never predicted); each throw plays on arrival. **Stacks:** the server burns every item; the screen plays at most ONE throw per 1.5 s, each standing for the items burnt since the last, with a count ("Fuelled 37 of 120 Bones"); the XP float shows only what each settle paid. **Refusals SAY WHY:** "Brannoc is hunting in the Sewer; the Hearth is in town." / "Den Knot needs Inner Flame 20." / "Logs only warm a flame below 15 (0.6 × your Level 25); bones take it higher." **Peers see** the hero at a fuel stand with the throw pose and a flare; for an everyday fuel, no item name (it is not their business); for a named fuel, the colour, sound and chat line. Mocks: `4-storyboard-fuel-1440.png` (4 frames), `5-named-fuel-1440.png`.

**Burn effect (new art, for the art director; not generated here).** (a) Fuel chunk: one 16 × 16 art px world sprite per fuel family (bone, jelly, husk, pith, log, coal) plus one per named fuel, class `small`; never the UI item icons (a different art set: never mix). (b) Throw: 3 frames, an arc from the hero's hand into the firebox mouth, 300 ms. (c) Flare: 6 frames at 10 fps, 64 × 64 art px centred on the firebox, from the palette's hearth ramp (`src/data/world/palette.js`, warm hues only); the hearth idle fire is its own 8-frame loop at 8 fps. (d) Named colour: the same flare and idle loop, palette-swapped per fuel (5 ramps above), plus 12 drifting ember motes; 60-90 s, fade over 3 s. The hearth's light is the warmest thing on screen (night: the square's light source).

**The shared flame: my "no bonus" call is OVERRULED by Tyler (10-25): "the hearth should give server-wide buffs eventually, maybe an XP buff to start."** The first build ships the visible flame only (its size shows the square's last hour of offerings; named-fuel colours are the shared moments). The bonus is the later feature below, designed so that each of my three objections is answered.

**Built (2026-10-27, lane/b-inner-flame; designer calls where the plan was silent).** Skill id `inner_flame` (Prayer's key renamed in place, no alias; `prayerSpeed` is `flameSpeed`). Everyday fuels run on the existing artisan bench ("Fuel the hearth" = one item, "Fuel all N" = the stack); named fuels burn alone through one server verb (`hr_hearth_burn`: catalogue XP, 20 a hero a UTC day, flame queue capped at 10 min, ledger row, the square's chat line, a quiet hero's burn unnamed). The flame is one per main map, server clock. Big Bones 15 → 60 XP so the ladder climbs (40/60/72/105). Named fuels gate at Inner Flame 1/3/5/10/19 (Comb, Den Knot, Rat King's Knot, Mosslight, Verdigris: each burnable by its own ground's hunter); paid after the dial 585/1,170/1,170/1,755/1,755. Rates moved from the proposals above to the Rare band the drop-tables guard enforces (10-20 h a piece per hero): Comb and Mosslight 1 in 770, Den Knot 1 in 400, Rat King's Knot 1 in 71, Verdigris Cake 1 in 80. The hive wasp keeps its silk (Crafting 1-9's only early source); Dry Husk drops from the cistern spider only. Not built yet: Clinker / Wisp Wax (no reachable ground), the logs/coal catch-up rung, the flame's size from the last hour, sound, the 3 s fade. "Ancient Claw" / "Void Chitin" wait for the naming pass.

### The Hearthrise: server-wide buff, later (2026-10-25)
(game-designer; Tyler 10-25 ruling; NOT in the first build; the balance sim measures it, and the pre-EA Security review covers it.)

| Objection | Answer |
|---|---|
| A quiet shard or a 4 a.m. player is weaker | **One meter for the whole SERVER**, not per instance; when it rises, every hero everywhere gets it at the same moment. |
| Upkeep becomes a chore | **An EVENT, not upkeep.** The server fills the Hearth toward a visible goal. When it fills, **the Hearth RISES** (a "Hearthrise"): the flame climbs the chimney, every town's square glows, a server line goes out, and every hero gets the buff for N hours. Then the meter resets to EMPTY and fills again. Nothing decays, nobody has a quota, not feeding costs nothing: the rise just comes sooner when more people play. |
| A contributable surface for no decision | Server-owned and journalled (below). The decision it adds: burn a named fuel now for your own Inner Flame burst, which also counts big toward the town's rise, or sell it. |

**What fills it: FUEL, never gold or Crowns.** Every item burnt by "Fuel the hearth" counts as points: bones and family fuels 1, big bones 2, dragon bones and higher rungs by tier (2-8), logs and coal 0 (they're catch-up only, so a gatherer can't fill the town), a named fuel 250 plus its colour moment. **Per-hero per-day clamp:** at most 300 points count from one hero per UTC day (about a day's bones from in-range hunting). Burning past the clamp still gives the hero its Inner Flame XP; it just stops counting toward the town's rise. No hero can fill the rise alone.
**Goal (proposal; the sim sets it):** **15,000 points**, about one rise a day with 60 active players at about 2 heroes each, slower on a quiet server. Rises never queue: points offered while the buff runs go into the next fill.
**The buff: +10% XP for 2 hours** (X and N picked against the pacing curve: `PACE.xp` 0.39 in `src/core/pacing.js`; L200 ≈ 4,000 h, ruled 10-19). One 2-hour rise a day is under 1% of a day's play at +10%: you feel it in the moment, but the Level curve doesn't bend. It applies to all earned XP (Level, combat skills, Inner Flame, gathering and crafting) on the server's settle; it never touches death loss. **Stacking:** there is no paid XP boost to stack with (final directive). Premium perks are not power (10-19). Blessings are death protection, a different axis, and stay unchanged. Any later in-game XP buff adds to this one, never multiplies with it.
**Server (§1, §6):** one row per rise kind (`hearth_rise`: kind, points, goal, rising_until, server clock); every counted offering in `player_ledger` (kind hearth_offer, points, the clamp state); the clamp, the reset and the rise are computed in SQL from the server clock. The client only reads the meter and the rise end time from the envelope. The edge engine reads the active multiplier server-side. It is a contributable surface, so it is in the pre-EA Security review (§3.7).
**Why it reads as Hearthrise's own, not OSRS's Well of Goodwill:** the Well is a gold donation in a menu. Ours is fuel won by hunting, thrown by your heroes into a fire everyone can see in the square, with named-fuel colour moments on the way, and it carries the game's own name ("the Hearth rose"). The IP rule allows the mechanic. Nothing of the Well's names, text or UI is used.
**Room for later rises:** the row has a `kind`, so later rises (gathering speed, drop luck) are new rows with their own goal, buff and fuel weighting. XP ships first. Show the meter as a ring of embers up the chimney, plus a number in the Hearth window; there's no leaderboard (not a ranked surface), and a hero sees only its own count today.

### New-player tutorial playtest (2026-10-24)
(game-designer, PLAYED; lane/d-tutorial-playtest; Tyler 10-24: "scale of 1-10 how is the tutorial for a brand new player?", mobile tester joining.) **How it was played:** the real client (`play.html` at main `cab1661bc` = live v0.2.42) in headless Chromium against a local server: the replayed migration chain (PGlite) answered every `/rest/v1/rpc/*` call as `authenticated`, and the real `hr-accrue` handler (`index.ts`, type-stripped by Node 24, PGlite behind a postgres.js shim) answered every engine call. Two brand-new accounts from the sign-in screen: Brindle at 1440x900, Wrenna in a touch-emulated 922x423 phone context. Only waiting was compressed, on the scratch database: the open hunt was moved back N minutes and the real engine settled it the way the world tick does. Key screenshots: `art/review/tutorial-playtest-2026-10-24/`.

**Score: 5/10 on desktop, 4/10 on a phone.** The bones are right: the card advances only on server facts, survives a reload, folds, and every step is real progress; hero creation explains "no class, the weapon is the style"; Hunts pre-picks the right ground with "Fits your level" and a "Before you go" check; going hunting is two presses; the fight is idle, readable and pretty; the analyzer gives real numbers. It loses points because no card rings a real control, two of the nine cards are never seen, one card names the wrong slot, the starter bar shows a dead spell from minute one, nothing teaches the town half (bag, shop, gear, cooking, farm, Tamsin), and a solo player's last card is a party gate that sits there for hours. **A 10:** Huntera's pattern with our depth: every card rings the control it names, also inside the window it opened; each card stays long enough to read; the chain covers the whole first-hour loop (go, fight, level, cash in, plant, cook, set the bar, take Tamsin's task, next ground, first gear piece); every card is doable alone; it hands off to a "What next" that always has a solo goal; and it reads the same on a phone.

**The walk (times are play time from the first screen):**

| # | Step | What happened | Stall / quit risk | Shot |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Sign in, Characters (0:00) | Create account, confirm email, sign in. A brand-new account reads "One can play at a time." and shows ONE empty slot: `hr_load`'s no_character answer carries no `hero_slots` or `account`. With a hero the same screen reads "Up to 4 can play at once" and two slots. | Low, but it contradicts the multi-hero pitch the last card makes | 04 |
| 2 | Create hero (~1 min) | Name, four colors, "How you fight" with the five styles. On the phone the five style lines are hidden (`src/styles/app.css:236` `display: none`); only the lead sentence shows. | Phone: never learns the styles | 05-phone |
| 3 | First town (0:01) | Card "Rats in the mill: Open Hunts and pick the Mill Cellar." sits under the icon row with a small caret at the first icon; nothing is ringed, the 16 icons have no labels (hover tip on desktop only), and a second card "Tally: Tamsin has kill tasks…" competes. Starter bar: 1 Challenge, 2 Flame Strike (struck: "Needs a Fire rune for each cast: you carry none"), 3 Power Strike, 4 Cooked Shrimp x20, 5 Salted Shrimp, 6 Thin Turnip Juice. | Medium on a phone (which icon is Hunts?) | 06 |
| 4 | Hunts (0:01) | Mill Cellar pre-picked, Enter in view. The card hides while any window is open (`src/shell/launcher.js` pointerFor). "Before you go" opens with "Runes: no Fire runes left. Flame Strike will not fire" on an untouched starter kit. | Low; the red line reads as "I broke something" | 07b |
| 5 | Enter (0:02) | The Hunts window STAYS OPEN over the hunt (phone: the sheet too), the hero is not in view and the card is hidden. 34 s later: 5 kills and Level 2, so closing the window shows card 4. Cards 2 ("Your hero fights alone") and 3 ("That is a level") were never on screen, on either device. | High: the two cards that explain idle combat and levels are lost | 08, 09, m08 |
| 6 | Mill Cellar wait (0:02 to 0:17) | "Out to the brook" (about a quarter of an hour, you can close the game) is good copy. Measured on the real engine: Level 5 after ~15 min, 4,118 Level XP/h, 1,128 gp/h of gold and loot, no food eaten. Nothing to do meanwhile: no card offers the farm, cooking or Tamsin, which all fit this wait. | Medium: 15 dead minutes at minute 2 | 09, 11 |
| 7 | Cash in (0:17) | Card plus caret at Leave hunt; "Leaving in 22s"; in town about 30 s later. | Low | 11 |
| 8 | Back in town (0:18) | Five overlays at once: the Hunt summary window, the analyzer ("Hunt ended"), the "Mill Cellar · Summary" bar, a "Start again" toast and the Tally card. The summary's primary button is "Hunt again" on the same ground, which pulls against the next card. | Medium: clutter, wrong way | 13 |
| 9 | Set the bar (0:19) | Card: "slot 1 is your own Cooked Shrimp … Press slot 1 and set the HP it eats at". Slot 1 is Challenge (v0.2.42 put the role spells first, `src/data/rule-subjects.js:91`). Pressing slot 1 opens "Cast Challenge whenever it is ready", no HP field, and the card is gone because a window is open. Any Save completes the step (the whole bar is stored and slot 4 already eats below 60%), so nothing is learned. Slot 4 itself teaches well ("At 60% you are missing 76 HP; 20 of its 96 would be wasted"). | High: wrong instruction, then a dead end | 14, 14-phone, 15 |
| 10 | Bag, Shop, gear (0:20, unprompted) | Inventory 22/100 with Sell junk. Shop: bought Leather Gloves (160 gp). The "Equip upgrades" offer appears at the bottom of the Shop under 12 seed rows, not beside the Buy that was pressed. Equip worked as one intent. | Medium: a new player may never see Equip | 20 |
| 11 | Professions (0:22, unprompted) | Gathering and crafting lists 8 skills, each with "Next at level N", but nothing says what you can make NOW from what you carry. Cooking's page is clear ("Cook Shrimp … Makes up to 10 · 3.8 s · 11 XP each") and ticks live. Entering a hunt stops it without saying so. No card ever points here (Andy a39). | High for professions: unfound | 21 |
| 12 | Thicket (0:23 to 0:48) | "Into the Thicket" works. The rares are named (Old Grayjaw, Alpha Fang), a real chase. Level 8 after 25 min (20,616 Level XP/h, 2,120 gp/h, 14 own Cooked Shrimp eaten). | Low | 26 |
| 13 | Last card (0:48 onward) | "Into the sewer, together": invite friends or make a second hero. For a solo hero, `currentCard()` still answers `go_below` at Level 24 (checked), so the "What next" goals (plant turnips, next ground, next unlock) stay hidden for hours. Tamsin's board has solo goals ("200 Gray Wolves … 480 gp + a Bronze Helm") but needs an armor-weight pick first, and Take is off with no reason while hunting (`src/shell/windows/tally.js:99`). | High: the guide ends at a wall for a solo player | 27 |

**Top 8 fixes, by first-hour value:**
