  | Club | Practice Club | as above | Forge Iron Warhammer (3 iron bars + 2 normal planks); two-handed, wear Melee 15 | `gear-tiers.js` warhammer twoHanded |
  | Bow | Practice Bow | Thicket from 5: leather helmet, pants, body, boots | Woodcutting normal_tree 1 -> 15, saw_normal to Crafting 15, oak_tree (15), saw_oak (15), String Longbow (3 oak + 2 silk: Cistern Spider 30%) | `gathering.js` TREES, `monsters.js` |
  | Crossbow | Practice Crossbow | as Bow | String Oak Crossbow (3 oak + 1 silk) | `gear-tiers.js` crossbow |
  | Wand + book | Practice Wand + Spellbook | Cellar Hive Wasp silk 15% -> Crafting 1-9 -> Apprentice Slippers, Hat, Robe Bottom, Robe Top (7 silk), wear Defense 1 | Carve Oak Wand (2 oak + 1 silk), Bind Oakboard Spellbook (2 oak + 4 bat wing: Culvert Bat 70%), wear Magic 15; Adept cloth at Defense 15 | `monsters.js` hive_wasp, culvert_bat |
  | Staff | Practice Staff | as Wand | Carve Oak Staff (3 oak + 3 bone chips: Kobold Tosher 50%); Adept cloth | `monsters.js` kobold_tosher |

  Each step uses only what the steps before it give plus the start kit (500 g, the seven practice weapons; tools are optional speed). Not done: browser play (the server half is STAGED and the Work window is lane/b-town-skills).

- **2026-10-22, Level XP and training XP apart (designer, inside Tyler's 10-22 "divvy up level exp vs training exp" + "an option tab for premium players that shows skill exp").** Cause: the analyzer's "XP gained" / "XP per hour" and the picker's best summed the Level's XP with every combat skill's; two party heroes with identical Level XP (tyler and Fingerguns, 205,093 each) read different totals because their Melee and Defense XP differ by use. Rulings: (1) every player-facing XP figure is Level XP unless it says otherwise (analyzer Session + Per hour, hunt summary card, picker personal bests, combat log "gains N Level XP"); (2) training XP (Melee, Ranged, Magic, Defense) lives only in the premium analyzer's Training tab: a total, a total per hour, and one line per skill (its XP and its XP an hour); free characters see Level XP only; (3) no skill XP ever floats over a head: the "+XP" floater is and stays Level XP (Settings label "Level XP gained"); (4) "Raw XP per hour" becomes "Level XP per hour before bonuses" (premium); (5) personal bests are Level XP per hour, and the bests already folded on the old sum are re-folded once (2026-11-10-hunt-xp-split.sql). Party role bonus (Tibia shared-XP math) parked: Tyler 10-22 "working as intended".

**2026-10-21: Mosslight Glade, the solo ground for levels 16-31 (H11; lane/c-ground-16-31). Built, fitted and server-catalogued, but STAGED: it is not listed and the server refuses it until its style-1 art lands.** *The gap:* today a solo hero at 26-31 has no ground in band, and the Hunts window recommends the Mill Cellar at Level 31. *The place:* Tyler's workbook H-003. It lies upstream of the Thicket, where the Meadowbrook rises from springs under the Warrens' copper hill. The goblins wash their ore in the spring run, and the copper water makes the moss glow. The map has two banks with six clearings: Crow Field, Poachers' Camp, then over the goblins' sluice boards (the one 1-tile choke) to the Sluice, the Mosslight Springs, Rook's Hide (a ruined spring-house) and Vine Hollow, then back over the ford. It is a solo ground, and up to three more heroes may come. *Creatures:* mixed families, as ruled on 10-20.
- Carrion Crow (L16, common): it follows the poachers' kills. Arrows bring it down.
- Glade Poacher (L19): it hits hard and is fragile. It takes pelts off the Meadowbrook wolves.
- Walking Vine (L22, the tank): slow and woody. A blade or fire answers it.
- Goblin Sluicer (L26): it pans the Warrens' copper and is soaked, so fire hisses off it.
- Rook the Poacher (L29): he holds the spring-house and is the one rare carrier.

Of Tyler's workbook creatures, the Mischief Sprite, the Pixie Trickster and the Briarling were folded out as generic fey. Fey belong to the Thornwild, and Greenhaven is roads, greenwood and ruins.

*Four slots:* each creature has one junk row (Bone Chips, Wolf Pelt, Magic Essence, Copper Ore, wolf meat) and one imbuement item of its own: Crow Quill (Keen), Snare Wire (Life Leech), Mosslight Tendril (Mana Leech), Sluice Crust (Ward), Rook's Moss Charm (Element Ward). The ground drops all five gear slots, about one piece every 1.5 h per hero: studded boots, a longbow, an iron helm, an iron platebody and studded legs, so the Ranger's line gets its first source. The rare is Rook's Whistle, about every 15 h per hero.

*Evidence (real settle, six seeds, per hero-hour at each floor):* net gold rises from the Sewer's party at 2,248, to the glade solo at 2,907, to the Warrens' party at 3,428. Level XP rises from 36,579 to 55,075 to 147,321. A ladder-kit Melee at Level 16 lives the hour on all six seeds. A practice-sword hero in Thicket leather dies on one of six seeds, at 33 minutes. At Level 24 the glade gives more XP than the Sewer solo (59.8k against 46.0k) and less gold (3,436 against 4,318), so the two grounds compete on different axes. At Level 8, below its range, the glade costs gold and the hero dies. Levelling from 16 to 32 here takes about 23 h.

*Weak spots (named):*
- Magic Essence's crafting uses open at Level 60 and up, so at 16-31 it is a stockpile and vendor gold.
- The ranged and magic bag guides are unplayed.
- With the ladder kit, the bill at the floor is small (150-250 g/h). The margin minimum holds, but the glade is easy for an iron-clad hero.

*To open it:* the Asset Director paints the ground and draws the five creatures' walks (list below). Then the hunt loses `staged`, the map moves to WORLD_MAPS, and the catalogues are regenerated. `forest-cave staged-until-art` holds that pairing.

*Art owed (style 1, no paid generation in this lane):*
- `mosslight_ground`, a painted 60x40 picture from `src/data/world/maps/mosslight_glade.js`.
- Walk, idle, attack, death and corpse for `carrion_crow`, `glade_poacher`, `walking_vine`, `goblin_sluicer` and `rook_poacher`.
- Pixel icons for the six new items, which use a twin until then.
- **2026-10-22, the town skills get a door (designer; lane/b-town-skills).** Found: the rebuilt client could start only Bury all and Cook all, so Woodcutting, Mining, Fishing, Smithing, Crafting and Runecrafting sat at zero although the server runs them (`set_activity` kind `gather` and `artisan`; live `hr_tick_config.armed_channels = {gather}` pays gathering every tick, the one live `gather` pointer's watermark was 15 s old on 10-22 11:17 UTC). Ruling: (1) **no new window and no new system.** The existing Gathering and crafting window (G) is the door: press a skill in its ledger and it lists what that skill can do at the server's level, then the next two rows above it ("Needs Woodcutting 15"). Each row has Start. (2) **One press starts it; another switches.** No stop-first: the engine collects the old work before it switches. Stop is one press, from the window or the bag's work line. (3) **Gathering runs until stopped or a hunt starts, with the game closed too** (tick-paid). **A bench run uses the bag and stops when an input runs out** (paid on the stop, the next sign-in, or a hunt's start). The window stops it for the player when the bag should be spent, and on a long run it settles every 30 min. (4) **One path:** `src/shell/town-work.js` owns start, stop and the done words; the bag's Bury all / Cook all now use it too. (5) Rows show catalogue facts (seconds per action with the tool the server says you hold, XP each) and the server's bag ("40 in your bag", "Makes up to 5"). Buttons stay off while a press is in flight and when the server's level, bag or an open hunt say no. Farming has no door here: it is grown in plots. Known limits, handed off: **a bench has no "make N"**, so Forge Bronze Pickaxe with 62 bars makes up to 62 unless stopped (Systems: a server-held count on `set_activity`, Lane C). **Coal is level 30 in data** although the 10-14 ruling says first tiers including coal are gatherable at any level (needs the `hr_activities` catalogue regenerated, Lane C). **Bronze Arrows and the whetstones have `ammoPerShot: 0`**, so crafting them has no repeat demand yet (the ruling says arrows keep a per-shot cost; Systems data). Prayer stays in the bag (Bury all).
- **Walk-through, a fresh hero's first hour in town** (starting kit `start-kit.js START_INVENTORY`, every skill 1; each step priced by the server's own `computeAccrual` over the real catalogues, 2026-10-22). Every row is reachable from the rows above it:

| # | Step (window G) | Needs (from earlier steps / kit) | Server result | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Fishing → Shrimp Spot, 10 min | Fishing 1 (req 1) | +107 Raw Shrimp, Fishing 5 | input for 2 |
| 2 | Cooking → Cook Shrimp, 8 min | 117 Raw Shrimp (10 kit + 107), Cooking 1 | 93 Cooked Shrimp, 24 burnt, Cooking 9 | slot 1 of the starter bar eats own Cooked Shrimp before store Salted Shrimp (14 gp per use) |
| 3 | Woodcutting → Normal Tree, 10 min | Woodcutting 1 | +125 Normal Log, Woodcutting 6 | input for 6 |
| 4 | Mining → Copper Rock, 10 min | Mining 1 | +125 Copper Ore, Mining 9 | input for 5 |
| 5 | Smithing → Bronze Bar, 5 min | 125 Copper Ore, Smithing 1 | 62 Bronze Bar, Smithing 5 | unlocks Forge Bronze Axe/Pickaxe (req 3) |
| 6 | Crafting → Normal Plank, 8 min | 125 Normal Log, Crafting 1 | 125 Normal Plank, Crafting 4 | second input for 7 |
| 7 | Smithing → Forge Bronze Pickaxe, Stop after the first | Bronze Bar + Normal Plank, Smithing 5 ≥ 3 | a Bronze Pickaxe (the Shop sells it for 120 gp); faster mining from then on | the first tool you made yourself |

**2026-10-22: Hunts, Hunt prep and the Party finder (designer plan, from Tyler 10-22: "Hunt prep kind of seems useless or clunky", "Party finder could use a lot of work, other games do this way better", "the 'Hunts' page could use some work").** Evidence is code and data on v0.2.30 (origin/main 48b89744). It is unplayed in the pane this pass: the harness refused the Play press on a QA hero, so no screenshots were taken. Look and layout belong to the Art Director (Charcoal & Gold; a list of lines plus one detail page, never a wall of cards). This entry rules the function. Lane B = client only. Lane C = migration/RPC first (§3.3), then its client half rides B.

*Ruling: Hunt prep is merged away and its window is cut.* Choosing a setup belongs to the moment you choose a hunt, so it moves to the Hunts detail. Managing setups belongs where a setup is edited, so it moves to the action-bar plaque, which already shows and renames the setup. That frees one launcher icon and the P key. Density stays per ground on the picker (rulings H4), and the setup becomes per ground beside it.

**A. Hunt prep** (`src/shell/windows/hunt-prep.js`, `src/shell/readiness.js`)
- *Purpose:* go hunting in the right loadout and know nothing will stop the hunt early. That is a step of going hunting, not a task of its own, so the screen fails the purpose test.
- *Wrong today:*
  1. It sits off the path. To hunt as another setup: plaque button → Hunt prep → Use → Hunts (H) → pick → Enter. That is two windows and five presses. Enter sends whichever setup is in use (`hunts.js:292`, `s.preset`), so if you skip the detour you hunt the Sewer on your Thicket setup.
  2. Nothing is remembered per ground, so you repeat the same switch every trip, which breaks the first principle.
  3. The readiness check almost always says "ready". Every hero wears a weapon from the kit (`start-kit.js START_EQUIPMENT`), any store-backed slot counts as stocked (`readiness.js:65`), and the only telling figure, "about N h of gold", is computed only from a running hunt's analyzer (`readiness.js:78-80`). That is exactly when setups are locked and prep cannot act on it. The check also ignores the ground: no level band, no party need.
  4. Setups look alike. A row is a name, "N pieces of gear" and four buttons (`hunt-prep.js:58-65,99`). "Save here" overwrites a setup in one press with no confirm (`:149-151`).
  5. Gear is not live. The bar edits the setup in use as you go, but gear changes need "Save here", so a new helmet left unsaved comes back off on the next Use. That is a hidden repeated decision.
- *Good looks like:* RuneScape 3's bank presets and Destiny 2's loadouts (mechanics only). You know a loadout by its weapon and key-item icons, you put it on with one press from where you are going, and you save it from what you wear.

| # | Build | Server reads / writes | Lane |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **"Go as" on the Hunts detail.** The picked ground lists your setups as lines: weapon/role glyph, name, the bar's first four icons. Enter = `hr_hunt_preset_apply` (only if the setup differs from the one in use) then `hr_hunt_enter`, one press, chained as `settleTownActivity` is today. Default: the setup in use. | `presetsOf`, `settingsOf().preset` (both exist) | B |
| 2 | **Remember the setup per ground.** `hr_hunt_enter_for` / `hr_hunt_party_start` write the accepted `p_preset` to a new `hunt_density.preset` column (same row as the density pick, 2026-11-17-hunt-density-home.sql). The `hunt_density` section carries it, and #1 pre-picks it. No new intent. | new column + section field | C → B |
| 3 | **Setup management on the plaque.** The plaque's ✓ opens a small menu anchored to it, not a window: switch (town only), Save as new, Delete (asks once, as now), rename stays inline. Then delete the `hunt-prep` row in `shell-windows.js` and its renderer and launcher icon. Art Director designs it. | `presetsOf`; existing preset intents | B |
| 4 | **Gear follows the setup live**, as the bar does. While on a setup, equip/unequip writes that setup's gear on the server. "Save here" and its one-press overwrite are gone. | equip RPC writes `player_action_sets.gear` for the preset in use | C → B |
| 5 | **"Before you go" lines on the picked ground**, replacing the readiness checklist. One line per bar supply in slot order ("Food: 20 Cooked Shrimp of yours, then Salted Shrimp from the store, 14 gp each"), stamina left, gold. For a party you lead, one line per seat (see C #5). A line shows only when it informs; the all-clear is one line. | bag, `settingsOf`, `staminaOf`, `goldOf` (exist) | B |
| 6 | **Gold runway per ground:** "about 6 h of gold at your best pace here". `hunt_bests` gains the best run's supply spend per hour, and the line hides when there is no best. | `hunt_bests.supply_h` (new) | C → B |

**B. Hunts** (`src/shell/windows/hunts.js`, `src/shell/hunt-list.js`)
- *Purpose:* choose where the next hours go: what pays what I want (Level XP, gold, a rare), at my level, alone or in a party. Then go in one press.
- *Wrong today:*
  1. A line is a stack of up to 10 small text runs in one micro size (`hunts.js:145-157`, `windows.css:256-259`). It carries engine facts ("6 packs of 2 to 3 · back in 60 s") and leaves out what decides a hunt.
  2. **A ruling is not met.** "Hunt card and bestiary show roughly how common each is" (Tyler 10-18), but the card never names a creature. `hunt-list.js` reads MONSTERS only to find the boss for `partyNote`.
  3. The chase is invisible. No rare is named: Old Grayjaw's Alpha Fang (0.3%) and the Rat King's Crown Shard (1.4%) sit in `monsters.js` drops and nowhere on screen.
  4. A fresh hero reads "Best alone: No hunt of 10 min yet" and "Best in a party: No hunt of 10 min yet" on all 4 grounds: 8 lines that say nothing.
  5. The recommended ground is pre-picked (`hunts.js:172`) but never labelled. A note shows only when nothing fits.
  6. A party ground leads nowhere. The Sewer says "Best with a party of 2–4" (`hunt-list.js:146`) and offers no next step.
  7. The setup is not chosen here (see A).
- *Good looks like:* Monster Hunter World's quest board, a master-detail view: a compact list, then the picked quest's page with its target monsters, rewards and conditions and one Depart. Also Melvor Idle's combat areas, where each creature's level and drops are one look away. Mechanics only.

| # | Build | Server reads / writes | Lane |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Master-detail.** The list rows become lines: name, band against your level (in / above / far above, in words plus the token colour), a solo/party glyph, your best Level XP/h or "New", and "Fits your level" on `recommendation()`'s row. The detail page holds the theme, **who lives here** (each creature with common / uncommon / rare from its share of `HUNT_GROUNDS` spawn points), **the rare and its carrier**, your bests (one "Not hunted yet" when there are none), density, then A #1 and A #5, then Enter. On a phone, the list then the detail as a sheet. Creature pictures use the style-1 world idle frame at integer scale until the approved style-1 bestiary portraits land (Thicket wolves and Warrens goblins have no portrait row in `monster-art.js`; no style mixing). | data rows + `hunt_bests`, `hunt_density`, level (exist) | B |
| 2 | **Hunt prep absorbed:** A #1, #3 and #5 land here. | as A | B |
| 3 | **A party path from the card.** A party ground's detail (or any ground while you lead) shows "Find a party" (opens the finder filtered to this ground and your weapon) and "Bring your heroes" (your other characters not already in a party, one press each; own characters join at once, ruling 10-19). | `partyMembershipOf`, open sessions (exist); finder ground filter (C #1) | B |
| 4 | **The setup remembered per ground:** A #2. | as A #2 | C → B |
| 5 | **Seat readiness before Start with party.** Each seat says ready, or names the reason (no stamina, hunting elsewhere, away), from the same predicate that makes `hr_hunt_party_start` refuse `not_ready`. Strangers' readiness is not in the envelope today. | party view seat `ready` + reason code (new) | C → B |
| 6 | **"Heroes at your level made about N Level XP/h here."** A server median from every character's `hunt_bests` by ground and level bracket, no names, hidden under 5 samples, so an unvisited ground has evidence and not a guess. Last on the list: with 3 players it stays hidden. | read RPC `hr_hunt_ground_stats` (new, aggregate only) | C → B |

**C. Party finder** (`src/shell/windows/party-finder.js`, the Party window's second tab)
- *Purpose:* "I want to hunt a party ground now with heroes near my level who need my weapon." Find them or be found in one press, then be in the hunt.
- *Wrong today:*
  1. **A lone player cannot be found.** Listing needs a party (`hr_hunt_party_list` → `no_party`), and a party exists only after a first invite (PARTY_SPEC §1.1). The client has no other way to make one. So the players who need the finder most can only browse, and two lone browsers never meet.
  2. **A row lacks the three facts every group decision uses.** The read returns `{id, hunt, max, seats:{seat, weapon, name}}` (2026-10-29-party-security.sql:298-310): no levels, no destination, no leader. `hunt_id` is the last ground hunted, so a row reads "Last hunted X" or "Party of N" (`party-finder.js:89`). The server already accepts `p_hunt`, but the client sends null (`:113`).
  3. It is a snapshot. Search is one press into window scratch (`:109-118`): no refresh, no count, and stale rows get Join refused.
  4. It is buried: the second tab, behind Invites (`party.js` TABS), with no link from the ground that needs a party.
  5. The leader side is a bare on/off "Show in the finder" (`:76-81`): no ground, so a listed party says nothing about where it goes.
  6. The empty state is the common state in a closed game, and it never mentions your own heroes, the quickest party there is (up to 4 at once, free).
- *Good looks like:* FFXIV's Party Finder (a listing is a destination, open role slots shown as role icons, and a short line; you join for a role) and WoW's Premade Group Finder (a lone player lists themselves with a role and leaders invite them; filter by activity). Mechanics only. Join stays one press with no leader approval (PARTY_SPEC, ruled 10-19).

| # | Build | Server reads / writes | Lane |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Listings carry a ground and levels.** `hr_hunt_party_list` gains `p_hunt` (the ground the party is listed for, stored in `hunt_parties.hunt_id`; the leader's Hunts window pre-picks it at Start). The finder returns each seat's level and the leader flag, and the client sends the ground filter the RPC already takes. | `hr_hunt_party_list(p_hunt)`, finder seat `level`/`leader` | C → B |
| 2 | **"Look for a party here" for a lone hero.** One switch lists this hero for a ground, with its weapon and its server level. Leaders see "Heroes looking" under their listing and press Invite (the existing invite and toast). The entry ends on join, hunt start, sign-out or 30 min. Social surface, so it is in the reopen Security review. | `party_seekers` table + `hr_party_seek(p_slot,p_hunt,p_on,p_idem)` + seekers in the finder read | C → B |
| 3 | **Rows that decide.** Each row shows the ground, a level range (lowest to highest seat), the open seats as the roles still missing (from the weapon filter's own map), the leader's name and how long it has been listed. Join is the primary on rows that fit. Rows above your band are dimmed and say why (the floor stays a warning). | C #1 | B |
| 4 | **Live, and counted.** Re-read every 15 s while the tab is open (inside `hr_rpc_gate('party_hunt_view')`). The Hunts list line shows "2 parties listed" for a ground. The Party window opens on Find when no invite is waiting. | existing read | B |
| 5 | **Entry from the ground (= B #3):** "Find a party" on the Hunts detail opens this tab on that ground and your weapon. | C #1 | B |
| 6 | **An empty state that leads somewhere:** "No party for the Sewer right now. Look for one here (#2), or bring your own heroes: Brannoc and Wrenna are free." The free heroes come from your open character sessions. | open sessions (exist) | B |

## Fresh-hero play report 2026-10-22 (live v0.2.26, designer, played)
Played as a blind newcomer (never played an MMO or an idle game) on https://hearthrise.net/play.html, the QA account, a new hero **Brannoc** (slot 2), desktop 1350×862, 05:48 to 08:18 UTC (live v0.2.26; v0.2.27 shipped while I was away, so the +2 h return ran on it). Every number below was read off the page and, where marked "server", checked against the live tables (read-only). Not done: the phone-landscape look (the pane had no resize tool); the second hero (the 40,000-gold slot purchase was refused by the harness permission check, so "Two new heroes together" is discoverability only).

### What works well (keep)
- **The first two minutes.** Name + four colour rows, then town with one coach card: "Rats in the mill. Open Hunts and pick the Mill Cellar." First kill 3 s after Enter, Level 2 in 40 s. The card "Your hero fights alone. You do not click to attack" is exactly the sentence a blind player needs.
- **The Cellar → Thicket handoff.** Level 5 at 14:37 of hunting (designer measure was 14.7), and the card then says to leave and go to the Thicket. The Thicket looks and plays like a living hunt (wolves, clearings, "+34 XP" over the hero).
- **Personal bests on the hunt card** ("Best alone: 7,141 XP per hour · balance 1,859 gp per hour") after one 15-minute run.
- **Weapon tooltips name the role**: "Practice Bow: Wield it to fight as a ranger, shooting from a distance"; Practice Staff "as a healer, keeping your party standing"; Practice Wand "With the Practice Spellbook it makes you a mage". Equipping the bow swapped the action bar to the Ranger set (Hunter's Mark, Precision Shot) and the Character window to "Ranger" at once.
- **Bury all** on bones (context menu): 15 bones, about 70 s, "Done: +234 Prayer XP."
- **Sell all loot** with a confirm ("Sell 5 stacks? Sell for 143 gp"), gold 671 → 814 matched the server.
- Hunt summary opens by itself on leaving; the "All characters" (O) window is a clear one-screen view of every hero.
- Console: no errors in the whole session (53 warnings, all "[hunt-scene] no sprite for unit N (null): not drawn").

### Top 10 fixes, ranked by first-hour value
| # | Fix | Evidence | Sev | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|

### Returning player at +1 h / +2 h
**+1 h (07:13, game closed 06:15 → 07:13).**
- Character select shows "Brannoc Level 10" and his gold (1,957). It does not say whether he is still hunting.
- Play → straight back into the live Thicket hunt (01:00:31), still running. GOOD: the idle promise holds.
- The analyzer is the only record of the hour: 544 kills, XP/h 21,344, "Gold 2,589 · Balance 2,421", supplies "Cooked Shrimp (own) ×20 free · Salted shrimp (store) ×12 –" (the store's cost shows "–", and "shrimp" is lowercase).
- Server: +1,142 gp of coins since entry (814 → 1,956), so the "Gold" line overstates gold by 2.3× (it counts loot at shop price; see #3). Level 5 → 10, Level XP 2,314 → 23,332, Melee 7,059 XP, stamina 6.82 h. The UI agreed on stamina (6:49h) and Level.
- There is no "while you were away" moment: no recap, no level-ups listed, no "your hero ate all 20 shrimp and is now paying 14 gp each". Nothing explains what happened.
- HP now reads 179/240, correct for Level 10. So the 150 cap seen earlier is a stale client value that a reload clears (refines #3b).
- Next goal: the coach card says "Into the sewer, together. Invite up to three friends, then pick The Sewer." A solo newcomer has no friends online and nothing else to aim at. The real next step was in the bag: Leather Body ×2, Coif ×2, Chaps ×2, Boots ×1 dropped (equipment drops work). Nothing pointed at them except a "new" badge. Wearing them took four separate right-click → Equip menus (no "equip upgrades", first principle). Defense was still 0 XP before equipping.
- Would I come back? Yes, for the armour. But only because I opened the bag. The game itself said "go find three friends".
**+2 h (08:12, closed 07:16 → 08:12, now v0.2.27).**
- Still hunting the Thicket at 01:59:20, Level 12. The analyzer now reads "Level XP 42,848 · Level XP per hour 21,541". The server agreed: +42,335 since entry.
- Defense went from 0 to 4,265 XP within the hour of wearing the leather picked up mid-hunt. Equipping takes effect in a running hunt. GOOD.
- Store food: 13 Salted shrimp in 2 h. The leather cut eating almost to nothing.
- Leave → summary "02:00:10 · 1,097 monsters · Level XP 43,200 · Gold 5,386 gp · Balance per hour 2,598 gp". The summary also lists "Supplies used: 20 × Cooked Shrimp (from your bag), 13 × Salted shrimp (from the store)". GOOD, but Gold is about 2.2× the real 2,443 (#3c).
- Sell all loot: 1,896 gp, including 308 bones and the 450 gp Alpha Fang, with no warning. Gold 5,153. The Shop's Buy tab still offers only the axe and the pickaxe.
- Next goal: the same "Into the sewer, together" card. I entered The Sewer alone at Level 12. The card "The Rat King wants a party of two or more. Alone, you skip that hall." is clear and GOOD. The hero lived (lowest HP 115/260 in 2 minutes), and the tutorial ended there with nothing after it. Left him hunting the Sewer at 08:18.
- Would I come back? For Level 15 weapons ("Next at level 15: Iron Sword…" in the Character window), if I found that line. Nothing on the main screen says it. No death happened in 2.5 h, so the death popup was not seen.

### Combat styles: what a new player learns
- **Where they'd expect to choose:** at hero creation. It offers only a name and four colour rows. Then the Outfit tab, whose free outfits are named Knight, Berserker, Ranger, Sorcerer and Druid. That reads as a class pick, and it is also a Tibia-name problem (§10). It changes nothing.
- **What actually happens:** you start as "Melee (shield)" (under the name in the Party panel and Character window). Nothing says why.
- **How you find out:** only by hovering a practice weapon in the bag. Each tooltip names its role, and those tooltips are good. I realised at 06:01, 13 minutes in, by going looking. A player who never opens the bag never learns it.
- **Switching:** Equip from the bag's right-click menu swaps the role, the action bar and the Character window together, in town, at once. This is good. The bow left the shield worn.
- **What each style needs:** nothing in the game says it. Defense trains from blows taken in armour, and no armour exists for a newcomer, so Defense sat at 0 XP. The Character window's "Next at level 15: Longbow…" lines hint at weapon unlocks, but not at what Ranged is.
- **Party roles** (tank holds, healer keeps the party up) appear only in the sword, shield and staff tooltips. The Party finder's weapon list reads "No sword yet (Melee (shield))", "No two axes yet", "No bare hands yet (Melee)" and so on, which looks broken.
- **Fix (ranked #2):** one "How you fight" card in creation and on the first town screen (copy in the table above), and a "Weapons" line on each Character-window combat skill ("Ranged: hold a bow or crossbow; needs arrows or bolts").

### Two new heroes together
Not played: buying the 4th slot (40,000 gold from tyler) was refused by the harness permission classifier ("Real-World Transactions"). A real new account starts with 2 slots, so it would not hit this. Discoverability, from the first hero:
- The only up-front hint is the character screen's "Up to 4 can play at once." In the game, the "All characters" (O) window says "Right-click a player, or one of your character tabs, to invite. Party (Y) finds open parties." Nothing tells a newcomer why they would want two heroes.
- The Party window's Invites tab says "Right-click a player and choose Invite to party". It never mentions your own heroes (the party spec: own characters join instantly).
- In "All characters", Brannoc showed "Ready to hunt" while he was hunting. "Bag 126 gp" reads as gold but is the bag's sale value (the Inventory showed 562 gp of gold at the time).
- Our own idle heroes stand on the same arrival tile, so their name tags stack into one unreadable block.

### Blind-player glossary gaps (terms used and never explained)
Level vs skill level ("Melee 3" beside "Lv 3") · XP (v0.2.26 mixed Level and skill XP as "XP gained"; v0.2.27 says "Level XP" but never says what it is) · stamina ("7:59h", "Stamina: hunting time left", and that it refills in town) · "packs of 2 to 3 · back in 30 s" · Density / "As built" / "1 each" · "Best alone / Best in a party" · "balance" / "Balance per hour" · "DPS and HPS" (Party panel title) · "Costs are personal." · "store from 14 g each, paid as used" (which store? when?) · "Any food" · "Charm" socket · "Action set" / "Preset 1" · "Chase" / "Distance" / "Offensive · Balanced · Defensive" · "Keep at least ___ gold" · "Challenge" / "Power Strike" · Runes ("your attacks use it, one per shot") · Blessings ("Free until combat level 30": combat level is never defined) · Prayer ("earn favour") · "Premium" ("More detail with premium" with no explanation) · "Tier 1 · Common" · "imbuement" (not seen yet) · "Bestiary 3 of 129" · "rested" (server `rested_xp`, not seen on screen) · and the big one: that the hero keeps hunting while the game is closed.

### Decision: what the game now teaches (ruled and built)
**2026-10-22, teaching a newcomer who never played an idle game (designer, inside Tyler's 10-22 NPE ruling; lane/b-npe-teach).** (1) No class pick and no class words: hero creation carries a "How you fight" column (`src/data/how-to-play.js` HOW_YOU_FIGHT, held to `hunt-roles.js` WEAPON_ROLES): no class, the weapon you hold is the style, all six starter weapons are in the bag, equip another in town to switch; one line per style with what it does and what it uses (food; arrows or bolts; mana and drinks). The five free outfits are renamed for what they are (Plate Harness, Fur Wraps, Hooded Leathers, Scholar's Robe, Grove Robe; ids unchanged, labels are client-only) and the Outfit tab says "Looks only". (2) The idle promise is said once, at the first long wait (card "Out to the brook", ~15 min), and lives in Settings > Help: nine read-only notes (idle, closed-game hunting, stamina, weapon = style, food and drink, the bar, Level vs skills, several heroes, death). (3) Several heroes: the last guide card offers "make a second hero" as the way into the party-only Sewer (2 slots, 4 at once, own heroes join at once). (4) After the guide the pointer becomes "What next": the next ground by Level and the weapon skill's next unlock, from data. (5) Level-up: "Level N" rises over the hero when the server's Level rises (its own Combat text toggle; never on a mount). (6) "While you were away": one alert on return (10 min or more) from a residue stamp of server numbers: Levels, Level XP, gold, kills, still hunting or why it stopped. Not done here: a cooking hint (no client way to cook yet; Systems: "Cook all" on raw food like Bury all); the bar card (fixed by v0.2.28 store-by-slot, `storedBar`).
### 2026-10-21 Armour weights (K7, lane b-armour-weights): RULED
**Model.** Each worn piece's armour value (`defB`) feeds its own weight's line: heavy = Absorb (share of a landed blow), medium = Dodge (the blow misses outright), light = Ward (a flat amount off each landed blow). A mixed set is exactly the sum of its pieces, no penalty. Absorb and Dodge are `maxPm × P / (P + k × Level)` against the hero's OWN Level, so own-tier armour holds its worth and an old set fades. One order in `damage.js`: dodge, absorb, resistances (the element permille), ward, then the spell shield and HP; a total cap of 60% over the chain (dodge and absorb together, and a landed blow always keeps its share). Rows: `core-model.js ARMOUR_MITIGATION` (dodge 650/0.45, absorb 750/0.7, ward 1.5 per point, cap 600). Derived, never stored: `src/core/mitigation.js`, written on the hero's unit each swing boundary; the Character sheet shows the engine's Dodge / Absorb / Ward.
**`def` keeps one job:** the monster's to-hit roll, now Defense skill + the defence of gear OUTSIDE the weights (shield, cape) + buffs. Weighted armour left it: plate's defB put an armoured hero at the 5% to-hit floor from tier 3 up (an L40 Warrens party's Healer spent 0 g/h), so armour weight could never be a choice. Defense XP: a dodge pays the plain rate (not a shield block); a status tick (`dot`) pays none (Tyler 10-22).
**Evidence** (real settle, one seed, one hour, per hero; before → after; tier-1 four-piece sets at L8):

| Run | Deaths | Damage taken/h | Bill/h (margin) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Thicket solo L5, starter kit (no armour) | 0 → 0 | 7,748 → 7,748 | 290 (+573%) → same |
| Sewer party of 4 L8, tank heavy / ranger medium / mage + healer light | 0 → 0 | tank 8,923 → 11,676 | healer 540 (+419%) → 600 (+365%) |
| Sewer party of 4 L8, ladder kit (caster-floor reference) | 0 → 0 | tank 15,918 → 14,420 | healer 1,150 (+128%) → 1,000 (+165%) |
| Sewer two tanks + mage + healer L8 | 0 → 0 | 6,288 + 3,557 → 8,642 + 4,222 | healer 480 (+469%) → 560 (+387%) |
| Sewer solo Melee L8, ladder kit (profit-floor reference) | 0 → 0 | 14,485 → 14,665 | 1,550 (+101%) → 1,480 (+119%) |
| Warrens party of 4 L32 (band floor), own-tier sets | 0 → 0 | tank 8,889 → 31,052 | tank 432 → 1,296 (+143%), healer 0 → 1,280 (+146%) |

**Each weight is the best choice somewhere** (same runs, the hero swaps only its four armour pieces): heavy for the hero who takes the blows (Sewer solo Melee bill 990 vs medium 1,536 vs light 2,044; party tank's healer bill 600 vs 760 vs 1,040); medium for the Ranger (265 kills/h and 2,388 g/h net vs heavy 246 / 2,273 and light 249 / 1,697: no style penalty, ranged bonuses, dodge for stray blows); light for casters (Mage 346 kills/h vs 288/279; Healer bill 600 vs 750/800) and against many small blows (Cellar farming at L10: 913 taken vs 1,073 heavy).
**Watch.** Medium's own defensive niche (a dodged blow carries no on-hit effect) arrives with the first monster that applies poison or burn. drop-tables' Sewer coin mutant moved /1.8 → /2.2: the old coin now clears +25% by a hair.
**Defense XP for a holding tank is about −18%: intended** under Tyler's 10-22 ruling. Every blow aimed at an armoured hero still pays, hit or miss. Only the shield-block bonus (a miss on a shield-holder) now fires less often, because plate no longer turns blows aside.

### 2026-10-22 Coppercap Warrens at its floor (CONFLICTS item 2): RULED
**Cause, measured** (six seeds, real settle, L32, the own-tier steel set). The lone Melee lost money before K7 too: −38% median, at a 5% to-hit. After K7 it bills 39,312 g/h against 3,069 g/h income (−92%). The causes:
- Monster damage. Goblins are lv 34–52 with atk 22–30 against Defense 32, so 37% of blows land. At 5.4 a Hobgoblin hits up to 162, 35% of 460 base HP, past the CORE_NUMBERS addendum's 30% for a regular.
- Food price. The Warrens' store row was tier 4: Salted Swordfish at 1.50 g/HP, while every goblin is tier 3 (Lobster 0.83 g/HP; the Sewer's Shrimp is 0.19).
- Not the cap or the Level scaling. Steel absorbs 53% at 32, under the 60% cap.
- Density is not the lever either. Even one goblin at a time out-hits regen: density 1 still bills 12,960 g/h (−77%).

No small lever makes a lone 32 profitable: it needs about 5× less damage per kill.

**Levers, data rows:**
1. `supplies.js` Warrens tier 4 → 3. This is the item-2 row; its bag guide moves to Cooked Lobster. Staged as `2026-11-13-hunt-sessions-catalogue.generated.sql` (one row: hr_hunt_travel coppercap_warrens 4 → 3; md5 30932fb145738707ed3706fde44d6b9e).
2. `combat.js monsterDamageScaleByTier` 3: 4.4. This applies the addendum's own rule: a Hobgoblin's biggest blow becomes 132, 29% of floor HP.
3. `hunt-grounds.js` Warrens `soloFrom: 45`. The card now reads "A party of 2 to 4, or alone from Level 45" instead of "Alone or with up to 3 more". It is advice like groupFit; the ground stays enterable.

| Warrens, six seeds | Before K7 | K7 alone | Ruled |
|---|---|---|---|
| Party of 4 at 32, by weight (min margin of the worst bill) | +674% | +183% | +645% |
| Party of 4 at 32, ladder kit | +192% | −61% | +52% |
| Lone Melee, heavy, at 32 | −44% | −93% | −80% (not offered alone) |
| Lone Melee at 45 (soloFrom): heavy / medium / light | — | — | no bill / +33% / +129% |

Every other row of the K7 table is byte-identical after the change, since only tier-3 monsters and the Warrens' store row moved. Regression: drop-tables `warrens-floor` (party of four in the ladder kit at 32 and a lone hero at soloFrom, two seeds, every role ≥ +25%). Its three mutants are each caught: food back to tier 4 (tank −26%), blows back to 5.4 (+9%), and alone from 40 (−21%). hunt-floor-warning G2 covers the card copy and has two mutants.

### 2026-10-22 Store-by-slot re-measure (v0.2.30-int, lane b-v0230-balance): RULED
**Reference bar.** Under store-by-slot an empty stored bar runs START_BAR (Salted Shrimp under 60% HP, Thin Turnip Juice under 30% mana) at every ground, so `play-ground.mjs` heroes at the Warrens ate tier-1 shrimp and the Warrens bills read near zero. The measured hero now runs the start bar's three slots at the same thresholds, naming the ground's guide food and drink (`supplies.js BAG_GUIDE_BY_HUNT`, and the store row partnered to each). Tier-1 grounds are exactly START_BAR. The Warrens bar is Salted Lobster and Thin Carrot Juice; the Glade's is Salted Trout.
**Tier-3 blows 4.4 → 4.0** (lever 2 above). With real bills the tank eats at 60%, so the Warrens floor party in the ladder kit read −10% at its worst seed (+19% median). At 4.0 a Hobgoblin's top blow is 120, 26% of floor HP, level with tier 4.

| Six seeds, real bills | Lowest seed (median) |
|---|---|
| Warrens party of 4 at 32, ladder kit | +77% (+112%); 5.4 would give −55% |
| Warrens party of 4 at 32, by weight | +873% |
| Lone hero at 45: heavy / medium / light | no bill / +33% / +87% |
| Lone heavy hero at 40 | −36%, so soloFrom stays 45 |

The K7 table on one seed, real bills: every reference row is above +25%. The lowest are solo Melee in light armour in the Sewer (+31%) and the Sewer starter party's Healer (+56%). The caster floor's Healer is +168%. The solo Sewer Melee pays 1,214 in heavy, 1,816 in medium and 2,310 in light. Each weight still wins somewhere: heavy for the solo Melee; medium for the Ranger (net 2,279 vs 2,237 in heavy and 1,572 in light); light for the Mage (346 kills/h) and the Healer (bill 600 vs 750 and 800).
**forest-cave `glade-solo-floor` mutant recalibrated, rule unchanged.** The Sluicer and the Rook are tier 3, so 4.0, K7 and the trout bar mean a ladder-kit hero at 16 now survives Warrens-strength blows: atk 26, 30, 36, 42 and 50 each lived all six seeds, at 3–8k g/h. The floor still bites: at atk 60, 3 of 6 seeds die; at atk 72, all 6 die (1.4–30 min). The mutant is now atk 18 → 72.

**2026-10-22: the Bestiary and the collection ladders count what a player can meet, and no milestone pays gems (lane/c-monster-ladders).**

*The bug:* the Bestiary total and the milestones counted the whole 129-monster catalogue, but only 19 monsters stand on open grounds and they drop only 49 items. So hunter25/40/60/85/All and collect50 to collect125 could never be earned, and the Bestiary read "x / 134". The two gem rungs (hunterAll and collect100) also rolled back whole, because the wallet refuses gems.

*The ruling:* a rung is a share of the reachable roster, so a new open ground raises every goal by itself.
- Monster rungs are sixths of `hr_hunt_roster`. Today the goals are 4 / 7 / 10 / 13 / 16 / 19.
- Item rungs are fifths of `hr_hunt_loot_roster`. Today the goals are 10 / 20 / 30 / 40 / 49.
- Both rosters are generated from the open grounds (`hunt-grounds.js reachableOf`, written by gen-hunt-sessions). Staged grounds are out.
- Only kills and loot on the roster count toward a rung.
- No rung pays premium currency (Tyler L12). The gold is unchanged.

*For Tyler (money):*
- hunterAll loses its 25 gems and collect100 loses its 15. No rung pays gems.
- Possible non-premium extras, not built: a "Bestiary Master" title, or a trophy for the home shelf.
- The gold is re-sized to the time each rung takes. The old amounts were sized for a 129-monster chase and, re-laddered, would have paid 165,000 per character inside the first day.
- Measured on the real settle: play-ground kill rates and the drop tables. The modelled path is the Cellar to L5, the Thicket to L15, the Sewer's party to L32, then the Warrens. Time on each ground follows the Level guard's rate.

| Rung | Goal (today) | Hours to clear | Gold before | Gold now |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| hunter10 | 4 monsters | 0.2 | 2,000 | 200 |
| hunter25 | 7 | 0.2 | 4,000 | 400 |
| hunter40 | 10 | 3.2 (Sewer) | 8,000 | 800 |
| hunter60 | 13 | 3.2 | 15,000 | 1,200 |
| hunter85 | 16 | 23 (Warrens) | 25,000 | 2,500 |
| hunterAll | 19 | 23 | 50,000 | 4,000 |
| collect25 | 10 items | 0.2 | 1,000 | 200 |
| collect50 | 20 | 3.2 | 5,000 | 600 |
| collect75 | 30 | 4.5 | 10,000 | 1,000 |
| collect100 | 40 | 23 | 15,000 | 2,000 |
| collect125 | 49 (all three rares) | 60+ | 30,000 | 8,000 |

- New total: 20,900 per character (was 165,000).
- By about 24 h the hero has earned about 51,000 and received 12,900 from milestones (25%). By the end of the item chase (about 176,000 earned) the milestones add 12%.
- The gold still rises every rung.
- Not modelled: Mosslight Glade, which is staged. When it opens it adds five monsters and its drops, and the goals rise with them.

## Designer pass 2026-10-21 (v0.2.22)
Evidence: the real settle (`hr-accrue accrueHunt`, the `tests/hunt-core/fixtures/play-ground.mjs` runner, one fixed seed, one hour, per hero per hour), run on a scratch copy of the tree with each change planted. Not played in the browser this pass.

### 1. Caster economy (CONFLICTS 2026-10-20 item 1): RULED
**Found.** (a) The −48% came from a harness whose "mage" held a staff, so it was a second Healer (Mage = wand + spellbook since 10-19). With a real Mage the live data is worse: the Sewer party of four at 8 **wipes** to the Rat King in 2.2 min (starter kit), 12.9 min (ladder kit), 5.1 min (full set). (b) **Root cause:** the 10-19 core rebuild moved every food heal ×12 with the new HP pools (`core-model.js FOOD_HEAL_SCALE`) and left the mana drinks at the old grain: a Thin Turnip Juice restores 6 of a ~230 mana pool. Its 3-mana instant half lands, but a Healer drinks every 2-3 s, so each drink REPLACES the running over-time half (`consumables.js`) and that half never lands: 2 g per mana in effect, at the shared 2 s cooldown, so the Healer cannot keep up and cannot eat. (c) The Mage's only damage spell, Fireball, spends a Fire Rune (Runecrafting 45; no store row, by the K10 rule). A wand hero casts **0** spells an hour.

**Ruling (one idea: mana is the caster's supply, sized for the pool it fills).**
- Every drink moves ×12 like the food did, back on its authored rule "a drink restores what its tier's fish heals" (crafted 96/168/300/384/528/648 = the cooked fish heals; basics 72/120/216/288/396/480 = the salted fish heals).
- Store drink prices ×5/3 (ladder shape kept): 10/18/45/80/138/233 g. Tier 1 is 0.139 g a mana. The 10 g is fitted: the Healer, who buys the party's healing, must clear +25% at the Sewer floor in the ladder kit (the kit the drop-tables Sewer reference uses). Bill ≤ 2,636 / 1.25 = 2,109 g at 209 drinks an hour, so ≤ 10.09 g.
- Fireball spends no rune (mana only). Flame Strike and Flame Volley keep the Fire Rune as their crafted upgrade.

**Numbers, Sewer L8, party of four (Melee tank, Ranger, Mage with wand + book, Healer), bill per hero per hour and margin on the even income share:**

| Kit | Before | After: played / deaths | Income | Melee | Ranger | Mage | Healer |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Starter (practice weapons) | wipe 2.2 min | 60 min / 0 | 2,623 | 602 (+336%) | 170 | 210 | 2,890 (−9%) |
| Ladder (weapon, helm, body) | wipe 12.9 min | 60 min / 0 | 2,636 | 168 | 160 | 240 | **2,090 (+26%)** |
| Full own-tier set | wipe 5.1 min | 60 min / 0 | 2,958 | 98 | 130 | 160 | 1,260 (+135%) |

Every role clears the +25% minimum in the reference kits. Exactly +25-35% for each role is not reachable: income is split evenly and costs are personal (ruling 10-16), so the Melee, Ranger and Mage bills are a tenth of a quarter share. The Healer is the binding role and is fitted to the band. Healer arithmetic (ladder kit): Nature's Wrath 281 × 30 + Greater Mend 337 × 25 = 16,855 mana an hour; 209 drinks × 10 g = 2,090 g; 2,636 / 2,090 = +26%.
Other floors after the change: Sewer solo Melee +32% (no drinks, unchanged); Sewer solo Healer +88% and Mage +155% (ladder kit), +64% and +110% (starter kit plus a helmet). Mill Cellar solo Healer in the starter kit goes from −72% to +227%. The Cellar's solo Mage now casts. Warrens party at 32: Healer goes from −43% to +374% (full set). In the ladder kit the Healer is +29%, but the tank's tier-4 food is −81% (CONFLICTS item 2, still open).

**Exact rows (lane C: every lever is in a server catalogue, so it is not data-only):**
- `src/data/drinks.js` DRINK_ITEMS `mana`: turnip 8→96, carrot 14→168, tomato 25→300, pumpkin 32→384, goldenroot 44→528, emberfruit 54→648 (`v` unchanged).
- `src/data/supply-basics.js` BASIC_SUPPLIES (mana, price): thin_turnip 72/10, thin_carrot 120/18, thin_tomato 216/45, thin_pumpkin 288/80, thin_goldenroot 396/138, thin_emberfruit 480/233. SUPPLY_SHOP_OFFERS drink price list (its ceil rule): 14/26/63/107/184/315.
- `src/data/abilities.js` fireball `rune: 'fire_rune'` → `rune: null`.
- `tests/supplies-catalogue.mjs` B1: the three-quarters rule uses the ×12 grain for mana too (`const S = FOOD_HEAL_SCALE`); the `core-model.js` comment names drinks.
- `tests/hunt-core/fixtures/play-ground.mjs` kit: mage = apprentice/oak/willow/maple wand + apprentice_primer/oak/willow/maple spellbook in `shield`.
- Regenerate both catalogues as new dated files. `gen-hunt-sessions`: hr_hunt_basic_supplies 6 rows, hr_hunt_abilities fireball rune null, hr_hunt_limits drink_mana_per_min_max 1,620→19,440, hr_hunt_vendor_value turnip/carrot/tomato/pumpkin/goldenroot/emberfruit 4/8/19/32/55/94 → 6/11/30/50/86/157. `gen-alpha-catalogue`: hr_shop_supply_offers 6 drink rows. Edge redeploy (packed src/data).
- Checked on a scratch copy with every row above plus the harness fix (exit codes): supplies-catalogue 0, hunt-core drop-tables 0 (Sewer party +618%, rare every 14.3 h), consumables 0, abilities 0, roles 0, sewer 0, conditions 0. abilities.validate is clean, and gen-alpha accepts the rows.
- **Regression (Systems, same commit):** drop-tables `caster-floor`. The Sewer party of four in the ladder kit plays the full hour with 0 deaths and every role ≥ +25%; it is red on today's data (wipe at 12.9 min). Mutants: drinks back to 6 mana; Fireball rune back (Mage casts 0). The harness fix alone turns drop-tables red (rare-pace: Sewer party wipes), so it must land with the rows.

**Economy and exploit review.** The gold sink shrinks: party net is about +1,970 g per hero-hour against +758 for a solo Melee (2.6×). This party premium is flagged for Tyler's economy model. Magic XP is mana spent × 1.5 and stays bounded by spell cooldowns and live targets, so drinks buy no XP outside a fight. The drink cap is regenerated. The vendor now pays book value for crafted drinks (the price-list cap rose). The Shop sells no supplies, so buy-then-vend cannot exist, and the recipe-yield rule reads `v`, which is unchanged. Fireball opens no gold path. Prices are a money surface: they go on the reopen Security list.

### 2. New-player path, top 3 first-hour problems (data and engine walk-through; unplayed)
| Step | Holds / can reach (real rows) | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| Create hero | practice sword + shield worn; 6 starter sets in the bag, 20 Cooked Shrimp, 500 g (`start-kit.js`) | - |
| Mill Cellar (band 1-8) | the only gear row: Hive Wasp `leather_helmet` 2% (1.00 helmet an hour) | solo Melee/Healer/Mage 0 deaths |
| The Sewer, solo at 8 | the kit + about one helmet | **Melee dies at 2.4 min**; Healer/Mage survive only after ruling 1 |

1. **P1: the second ground kills a Melee newcomer.** The Sewer's +32% reference assumes a bronze sword + helm + platebody that nobody starts with. Practice sword + shield + helmet + platebody still dies (6.7 min). Practice weapons plus a full leather set survives at +42%. Proposal (Systems, hero-kit catalogue): a bound four-piece starter armour set in the kit. This keeps Tyler's Cellar → Sewer → Thicket order.
2. **P1: casters do not work** (ruling 1, staged as rows): a wand hero casts nothing, a Cellar Healer runs at −72%, and a Sewer party wipes in 2-13 min.
   **Ruled rows (addendum 2026-10-21, for a Systems lane: items.js, start-kit.js, the hero-kit catalogue, item-art, item-descriptions):** four NEW bound pieces, not `leather_*` granted (the leather rows carry rangeAtkB/crit and `leather_body`/`leather_pants` carry magicAtkB −1, which skews a role). Each row is `v: 0, bop: true, type: 'armor', armourClass: 'leather'` (medium = Dodge, the one weight that adds neither HP nor mana, so no role is favoured), `tier: 1, rarity: 'common', reqSkill: 'defense', reqLv: 1`, with defB only and no attack, crit or magic modifier:
   `practice_cap` "Practice Cap" helmet defB 2 · `practice_jerkin` "Practice Jerkin" body defB 3 · `practice_leggings` "Practice Leggings" pants defB 2 · `practice_boots` "Practice Boots" boots defB 2 (= the leather set's defence, which the starter-weapon Melee survives the solo Sewer hour in at +42%).
   They start WORN (`START_EQUIPMENT` helmet/body/pants/boots beside the practice sword and shield), so the first Sewer run needs no bag trip. ONE set serves all six weapon sets. Source: the kit only; no vendor value, bound, so no faucet. Art reuses the leather icons until the Art Director paints them.
   Also fixed on this branch: `supplies.js` BAG_GUIDE_BY_HUNT `millrace_sewer` magic ammo is 1,500 Fire Runes → the free Air Rune rung (no Sewer spell spends a rune now).
   **Superseded by Tyler 2026-10-21:** a fresh solo Melee starts in Meadowbrook Thicket; the Sewer is the first party ground (the spider nerf is withdrawn). The tutorial chain is re-sequenced to match (`src/data/hunt-tutorial.js`). Walk-through: a solo start-kit Melee on the real settle, armour lane's kit.

   | # | Card | Player does | Server fact that completes it | Minutes |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | 1 | to_the_cellar | opens Hunts, picks Mill Cellar | hunt session on `mill_cellar` | ~0.5 (a click) |
   | 2 | first_kill | watches | kill count +1 | 0.05 (3 s) |
   | 3 | first_level | keeps hunting | a level rose (Level 2) | 0.3 |
   | 4 | earn_the_thicket | keeps hunting | Level ≥ 5 (Thicket floor) | ~14 more (Level 5 at 14.7) |
   | 5 | leave_cellar | Leave hunt | session ended `left` | ~0.5 (next settle) |
   | 6 | set_the_bar | Any food + My HP % below 60 | the stored bar holds a conditioned slot | ~1 |
   | 7 | into_the_thicket | picks Meadowbrook Thicket | hunt session on `meadowbrook_thicket` | ~0.5 |
   | 8 | earn_the_sewer | keeps hunting, 0 deaths in 3 h | Level ≥ 8 (Sewer floor) | 23 (Level 5 → 8) |
   | 9 | go_below | forms a party, picks The Sewer | hunt session on `millrace_sewer` | party-dependent |

   About 40 minutes of hunting to card 9. The two Sewer pull cards (first room, Rat King) are cut. The King's pocket is `minParty: 2`, so a solo hero could never finish that card. He becomes card 9's party goal in its text. The walk-through test now refuses a pull card on a party-only pocket. Mutants: the King card back; the Thicket skipped. The old card said "fire glances off him", but the engine has no basis for it: abilities use element `'fire'`/`'nature'` while monster rows and `combat.js` use `'ember'`/`'frost'`/`'poison'`, so Fireball ignores the King's ember resist. That's a Systems bug, and the text was dropped.
3. **P2: the guided path ends after card one.** `src/shell/launcher.js` shows only `HUNT_TUTORIAL[0]`. The eight later cards never show: Sewer unlocks at 8, Leave to cash in, set the bar (Tyler: "the tutorial teaches the condition builder"), the Rat King. After the first kill nothing says what to do next. Hand-off: Systems (wiring) + Art Director (coach card).
